  - `src/areas/households/components/`: the `family` folder whole (`FamilyCard` and `FamilyRegistrationPanel`).
  - `src/areas/admin/components/`: the `admin` folder whole (11 files) and `src/components/AdminAnnouncementPanel.tsx`.
  - `src/areas/governance/components/`: the `governance` folder whole (`GovLayout`, `GovernanceDocuments`, `OfficerPhoto` and `RichTextEditor`).
  - `src/areas/clubs/components/`: the `maps` folder whole (`ClubLocationPicker` and `LCAMap`).
  - `src/areas/membership/components/`: the `uscf` folder whole (`UsChessMembership` and `UscfSearchInput`).
  - `src/areas/registration/components/`: `src/components/RegistrationReminderButton.tsx`.
  - Unchanged in `src/components`, the pieces every area uses: `ui/`, `layout/`, `errors/`, `auth/`, `PageHero`, `StatusBadge`, `FacebookFeed`, `DonateButton`, `AnnouncementBanner`, `ImpersonationBanner`, `FilterDropdown` and `MultiSelectDropdown`. Every moved file keeps its name. The area `index.ts` files do not change (see Decisions, "Components by area").
- **The run.** `--dry-run` on the map: 18 moves (34 files), 59 specifiers in 27 files, 486 files searched, 0 that could not be resolved. Applied with the same script as steps 16 and 17 and no change to it. Every specifier it rewrote is an `@/` alias: 57 static imports (in 18 pages and 8 of the moved components) and 2 dynamic `import()` calls, the lazy `RichTextEditor` in `GovernanceDocuments` and the `FamilyRegistrationPanel` import in `test/unit/tournament-pages-pricing.test.tsx`. `src/App.tsx` imports only shared pieces (`auth/`, `layout/`, `AnnouncementBanner`, `ImpersonationBanner`) and lazy-loads pages, so it did not change, and no shared component imports a moved one.
- **Path mentions fixed by hand.** The header comment naming its own old path in 31 of the 34 moved files (`AdminAnnouncementPanel`, `AgendaList` and `MiniCalendar` have none), and five pinned paths in two tests: four lines of `test/unit/client-sections-audit.test.ts` (`FamilyRegistrationPanel`, `TournamentWizard`, `SectionRulesEditor` and `PrizesEditor`) and one of `test/unit/no-member-price-copy.test.ts` (`FamilyRegistrationPanel`). No test mocked a component path: `vi.mock` and `vi.doMock` of `@/components/` matched nothing under `test/unit` before the move.
- **`verify-move`** against the commit before the move, with `--ignore REDESIGN_STATUS.md --ignore docs/redesign/REDESIGN_SPEC.md --ignore eslint.config.js` (the third because the rules below change it in the same commit; see Deviations): 34 renames (11 specifier lines and 31 path mentions inside them), 21 other files (48 specifier lines and 5 path mentions), nothing else. The map, the two new tests and the four lint fixtures are new and listed as untracked.
- **The boundary rules,** in `eslint.config.js`, written like the `domain/` block (`no-restricted-imports` with `patterns`, a message per pattern):
  1. **An area never imports a page (error).** For `src/areas/**`, `no-restricted-imports` refuses `@/pages`, a relative path that climbs to `pages`, and any path through `src/pages`, type imports included. Nothing imports a page today.
  2. **One area reaches another through its `index.ts` or `api.ts` (warning).** One config block per area folder, with the list of areas read from `src/areas` when the config loads, so a new area is covered without editing the config. Inside `src/areas/<area>`, an import of another area by alias or relative path warns unless it names the area's folder, `index` or `api` (with or without an extension); imports of the file's own area are not limited, and type imports and `export ... from` count. The rule is carried by `@typescript-eslint/no-restricted-imports`, the same rule with the same options, so it can warn on files where rule 1 is an error (see Deviations).
  3. **Pages do not call fetch (warning).** For `src/pages/**`, `no-restricted-globals` flags `fetch`, and `no-restricted-properties` flags `window.fetch`, `globalThis.fetch` and `self.fetch`. A method called `fetch` on another object, or a function such as `fetchTournaments`, is not flagged.
  The fixtures sit in `test/unit/fixtures/lint`, which the default lint run already skips for the `domain/` fixture.
- **What the rules find today.** Rule 1: nothing. Rule 2: five imports across areas, four of them components (`RichTextEditor`, `SectionRulesEditor`, `UsChessMembership` and `GradeConfirm`) and one a library file step 17 moved (`@/areas/events/champions`). Rule 3: four `fetch` calls in three pages, all to `/api/clearinghouse`. Each is listed under Follow-ups with its file, line and import, and `area-boundaries.test.ts` fails if one is added or fixed without the list changing.
- **Route chunks.** `npm run build` on a copy of the commit before the move and on the moved tree: the same 115 files in `dist/assets`, and `diff -r` of the two `dist` folders finds no difference, so the built site is byte for byte the same (as in step 17; unlike step 18, no hash changes). Vite transforms 2413 modules in both. The component chunks keep their names because their files do: `GovLayout`, `GovernanceDocuments`, `LCAMap`, `PreviewBanner`, `PrizeWinners`, `RichTextEditor`, `SectionRulesEditor`, `TournamentList` and `UscfSearchInput`, with `PageHero`, which stays. The full list of names is in step 17.
- **Every lazy route resolves.** `test/unit/app-routes.test.tsx` reads each `lazy(() => import('@/pages/X').then((m) => ({ default: m.X })))` in `src/App.tsx`, checks it found as many as there are `lazy(` calls (33, all under `@/pages`), then imports each page module the way the route does (under jsdom, with the Supabase client mocked) and checks its named export is a component. A page that imports a component by a path that no longer exists, or a renamed export, fails there rather than when someone opens the page. `test/unit/render-smoke.test.tsx` is unchanged and passes.
- **Thin pages.** New and rebuilt pages compose area components and call area `api.ts` modules (see Decisions, "Thin pages"). The large pages are left as they are; the workstreams that rebuild them split them up.
- **Merge order.** Ordering rule 3 now covers the moved component folders and files too.
- **The brief's component paths are now out of date.** `REDESIGN_SPEC.md` names components under the old folders, for example `src/components/tournaments/Crosstable.tsx`, `src/components/family/FamilyRegistrationPanel.tsx`, `src/components/uscf/UscfSearchInput.tsx`, `src/components/admin/TournamentWizard.tsx`, `src/components/maps/LCAMap.tsx` and `src/components/governance/GovLayout.tsx`, and places new ones there (`src/components/admin/FestivalsPanel.tsx` and `AdminSidebar.tsx`, `src/components/family/AddFamilyMemberForm.tsx`, `src/components/governance/NextMeetingCard.tsx`). See Decisions, "Components by area".
- Checks: `npm run lint` is 34 errors before and after, the same errors on the same lines once the moved paths are mapped; the warnings go from 4 to 13, the 4 `exhaustive-deps` warnings as before plus the 9 the new rules raise (see Decisions, "Area boundaries and the lint baseline"). Sixteen of the 34 errors are in moved files and stay (see Deviations). The new tests and the config lint clean. `npm run build`, `npm run check:bundle` (no zod in 115 files), `npm run typecheck:functions`, `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` and `npm run test:all` pass: unit 1601 in 74 files (1550 in 72 before: 17 in `area-boundaries.test.ts` and 34 in `app-routes.test.tsx`, one per route plus the one that reads `App.tsx`), integration 790 in 58 files (unchanged). `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/`, `src/lib/scanner` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed.

### Checkpoint C: steps 16 to 19

**Merged into `main` as `7151bff` on Tuesday, October 13, 2026 at 2:03 AM UTC** (pull request 107, a merge commit on head `cedb39f`), after a three-part review of the built site, the Pages Functions bundle and the daily-emails worker found them identical in behaviour to `748a5ef`, with only module order and file paths changed, and nothing to fix. The Pages production build, the worker redeploy and the main branch checks all succeeded on the merge commit, and the public site answered afterwards with the same pages and sizes as before. Checkpoint C groups the code by area and changes no behaviour. Every step is a scripted move or split, checked by a second script against the commit before it, and the built site is the same.

- **Step 16:** the server helpers move into `functions/utils/events`, `registration`, `membership`, `live` and `admin` (`scripts/moves/server-areas.json`, 20 files), with the two tools the later steps reuse, `scripts/move-files.ts` and `scripts/verify-move.ts`.
- **Step 17:** nine site library files move into `src/areas/events`, `clubs` and `admin` (`scripts/moves/client-lib.json`), and each of the eight area folders gets an `index.ts`.
- **Step 18:** `src/lib/api.ts` is split into `src/lib/http.ts` and one `api.ts` per area (`scripts/split-api.ts` and `scripts/moves/api-split.json`); `src/lib/api.ts` stays as a barrel, so no importer changed.
- **Step 19:** the domain components move into `src/areas/<area>/components` (`scripts/moves/client-components.json`, 34 files), and three lint rules keep areas from importing pages, areas from reaching into each other's files, and pages from calling `fetch`.
- **What the merge does to production.**
  - No migration. Nothing under `migrations/` changes, so `migrate-db.yml` finds nothing to apply and production stays at `0054_requires_lca_membership.sql`. The preview database needs nothing either.
  - Pages rebuilds the site. The step 17 and step 19 builds are byte for byte the same as before them; step 18 changes the hash of 46 files but no file name, and no size by more than 0.01 kB. The server functions bundle differs only in module names and path comments.
  - `.github/workflows/deploy-worker.yml` runs on any change under `functions/utils/**`, so the merge redeploys the daily-emails worker. Its code is the same; only the paths it imports from changed (`campaigns`, `membershipExpiry` and `ratingSnapshots`, step 16).
  - Nothing visible changes. No switch in `src/lib/features.ts` changes, and no endpoint, request or response changes.
- **Before merging.** Ordering rule 3: no other branch touches `functions/utils`, the moved `src/lib` files, `src/lib/api.ts` or the moved component folders while the checkpoint is open. A branch that does is rebased after the merge and its imports moved to the new paths; `server-areas.json`, `client-lib.json` and `client-components.json` under `scripts/moves` name each old path and where it went.
- **Rollback.** `git revert -m 1 <merge sha>` on `main` and push. There is nothing to undo in a database, and the revert redeploys the worker again with the old paths.
- **Checks at the end of the checkpoint.** `npm run lint`: 34 errors and 13 warnings (34 and 4 before the checkpoint; the 9 new warnings are the follow-ups the boundary rules list). `npm run build` (115 files), `npm run check:bundle` (no zod), `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run test:all` pass: unit 1601 in 74 files (1457 in 67 before the checkpoint), integration 790 in 58 files (unchanged through the checkpoint).

### Step 20: browser tests for accessibility and end to end

The first step of checkpoint D, the design system (steps 20 to 32). It adds the browser layer every later design step is tested against. The base was `a25c4a5`, the commit that recorded checkpoint C; nothing is committed yet.

- **Files.**
  - Packages: `@playwright/test` 1.63.0 (published Friday, September 4, 2026) and `@axe-core/playwright` 4.13.0 (Tuesday, August 11, 2026), both exact-pinned and over two weeks old (Playwright 1.64.0 was 6 days old, so it is not used). An `overrides` entry holds `playwright-core` at 1.63.0, because npm otherwise put 1.64.0 at the top level to satisfy axe's open peer range. New scripts `test:a11y` and `test:e2e`; neither is in `test`, `test:all` or the CI `check` job.
  - `playwright.config.ts`, `test/a11y/global-setup.ts`, `test/a11y/support.ts`, `test/a11y/smoke.spec.ts`, `test/a11y/builds.spec.ts`, `test/a11y/seeded-data.spec.ts`, `test/a11y/axe-detects.spec.ts`, `test/a11y/baseline.ts`, `test/a11y/baseline.json`, `test/a11y/known-violations.json`, `.dev.vars.a11y`.
  - `test/e2e/register-family.spec.ts`, `test/e2e/publish-round.spec.ts`, `.github/workflows/e2e-preview.yml`, and an `a11y` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (pull requests only; it installs Chromium with `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium` and runs `npm run test:a11y`; the `check` job is unchanged).
  - `scripts/seed/fixtures.sql`, a header comment in `scripts/db/preview-guard.ts` (no code change; its `--seed` flag now loads the real file), `.gitignore` (adds `dist-a11y-on/`, `dist-a11y-off/`, `.wrangler/a11y/`, `test-results/` and `playwright-report/`), and a "Browser tests" part of `README.md`.
  - `src/lib/features.ts`, `src/App.tsx`, `src/pages/fixtures/ScopeFixturePage.tsx`, `src/vite-env.d.ts`, `eslint.config.js`.
  - Six new unit files and a helper: `test/unit/feature-overrides.test.ts` (15), `seed-fixtures.test.ts` (14), `seed-fixtures-edges.test.ts` (53), `e2e-preview-guard.test.ts` (60), the test of the early stop's shell script (47, the other `e2e-preview-` file in `test/unit`; named under Deviations), `browser-test-wiring.test.ts` (83) and a small helper shared by those last three (in `test/unit/helpers`); `test/unit/app-routes.test.tsx` gains one case.
- **The three Playwright projects.**
  - `a11y-on` (port 8801) and `a11y-off` (port 8802) each run against their own build. A `globalSetup` cannot do the build: Playwright 1.63 starts the web servers before `globalSetup`, so `npm run test:a11y` runs `test/a11y/global-setup.ts` first and then `playwright test --project=a11y-on --project=a11y-off` (see Deviations). The setup builds both variants with every `VITE_*` value set on the command line (including a fake Supabase address, `http://127.0.0.1:9`, and an empty Maps key), deletes `.wrangler/a11y`, applies the migrations with `wrangler d1 migrations apply lca-db --local --persist-to .wrangler/a11y` and loads the seed with `wrangler d1 execute --local`.
  - `e2e-preview` takes its address from `E2E_BASE_URL`. `checkE2EBaseUrl` stops the run for an empty value, a value that is not an http or https address, `louisianachess.org` and any subdomain of it (any case, port, trailing dots, a user name and password before the host, a backslash or an ideographic dot), the Pages production addresses `lca-website.pages.dev` and `main.lca-website.pages.dev`, and a single deployment's own address, `<8 hex characters>.lca-website.pages.dev`, which can run on production data. The config reads `--project` in its main process, so the check runs only when `e2e-preview` is selected and the web servers start only for the two a11y projects.
  - `PW_CHROMIUM_PATH`, when set, becomes the browser's `executablePath`; it is needed only in this container (see Verify items).
- **The a11y servers and their secrets.** `wrangler pages dev` runs each build with the local database, `--persist-to .wrangler/a11y` and its own inspector port (two servers can share one persist folder only that way). `.dev.vars.a11y` is committed and holds fake values only (its header says so): `SUPABASE_URL` points at `127.0.0.1:9` so any sign-in fails at once, addresses are `example.org`, and `SITE_URL` is local. The config passes each of its lines as a `-b NAME=value` binding, which overrides `wrangler.toml` `[vars]` and any `.dev.vars` on the developer's machine. It also binds `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `RESEND_API_KEY`, `FACEBOOK_PAGE_TOKEN`, `FACEBOOK_PAGE_ID` and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` to the empty string (`A11Y_EMPTY_SECRETS`), refuses a `.dev.vars.a11y` that sets one of them, and starts the servers with `CLOUDFLARE_INCLUDE_PROCESS_ENV=false`. A scratch run with a planted `.dev.vars` carrying sentinel values showed the empty bindings win (length 0), and the control run without them carried the sentinels. `test/unit/browser-test-wiring.test.ts` walks `functions/` for every `env.X` name and requires each to be in `.dev.vars.a11y`, in `A11Y_EMPTY_SECRETS`, or to be `DB` or `CLUB_LOGOS`. `test/a11y/support.ts` seeds `Math.random`, stubs `/api/facebook-posts` with `page.route` and blocks and records any request to a host that is not local; the smoke spec fails on one. An empty key means a call to the real service is refused, but a checkout or an email can still send that refused request (see Follow-ups).
- **The seed and its guard.** `scripts/seed/fixtures.sql` is synthetic only: every id starts with `fx-`, every email and link is at `example.org`, every person is "<first name> Fixture", nobody has a US Chess ID, and dates are relative to the day it is loaded. It was written against the schema after migration 0054 and loads on a database built from migrations 0001 to 0054, with the 0053 triggers filling the section and schedule rows from the JSON in `tournaments.sections` (the triggers never write `fee_early` or `fee_late`, so the early and late prices are set by an `UPDATE` after the insert).
  - `fx-t-open`: an open LCA event with object sections (Open at 40, 32 and 50, Under 1600, Scholastic) and an event-level early discount and late fee; `fx-t-quads`: an event with a `round_schedule` (rounds at 10:00 AM, 11:30 AM and 1:00 PM); `fx-t-legacy`: sections that are plain strings; `fx-t-done`: a completed event with `1/2-1/2` draws, a full bye and two half-point byes, whose standings contain a tie; `fx-partner-1`: a partner event in `clearinghouse` with no entry rows.
  - Two clubs, a pinned news post and one more, three governance documents, two champions, two board seats with holders and a region, and a guardian with two children. The children's `members.email` is the guardian's address, as `POST /api/me/children` does, and their registrations store the confirmed range `0-12`, never a grade.
  - It can be loaded again: clubs, members, seats, posts, documents and champions are upserted, the fixture tournaments and their entries are deleted and re-inserted, deletes touch only `fx-` rows, and there is no `BEGIN` or `COMMIT`. `npm run db:seed:preview` still runs `scripts/db/preview-guard.ts --seed` first and loads the file only after the guard passes; it still refuses a placeholder preview id.
- **The preview run and its early stop.** `.github/workflows/e2e-preview.yml` has `workflow_dispatch` as its only trigger, read-only permissions, and five required boxes plus the preview address as inputs: the preview database id is in `[env.preview]`, the preview is migrated and seeded from the branch, the preview holds Stripe test keys, WS06's household checkout has shipped, and WS07's round publishing has shipped. Its first job reads the host with the runner's own `node` (`new URL`, lowercased, trailing dots removed, https assumed only when there is no scheme at all) and exits 1, before `npm ci` or Chromium, unless every box is ticked and the host is not the live site, a Pages production address or a single deployment's address; the `e2e` job needs it. A unit test extracts the real shell script and runs it under `bash -e` for each combination of boxes and addresses, including a trailing dot, a user name and password, a missing scheme, a leading space and `%2E`. The `e2e-preview` project runs with trace, screenshot and video off, since a trace records every typed value, and the run uploads `playwright-report/` only when a job fails and never its `data/` folder, so a test login can never land in a public artifact. Both specs are `test.fixme` with their preconditions and steps written out, so a run reports them as skipped. The family spec reads `FAMILY_MEMBERSHIP_CHILDREN` from `domain/households/family` rather than writing a number, and asks "In 12th grade or below?" because the seed's Scholastic section has `gradeMax` 12.
- **Switches in the a11y build only.** `src/lib/features.ts` keeps its values as `SWITCHES`, and `FEATURES` is `SWITCHES` in every mode except `a11y`, where `applyFlagOverrides` turns on the names in `VITE_FLAGS` (a comma-separated list; an unknown name throws, so a typo fails the run). The mode check is a literal `import.meta.env.MODE === 'a11y'`, so other builds drop the override. `ScopeFixturePage` (`base`, `heritage`, `scholastic` and `live`, each setting `data-theme` on its container) is a lazy import in `src/App.tsx` only in that mode, at `/__fixtures/:name`. `feature-overrides.test.ts` builds production into a temporary folder with `VITE_FLAGS` set and finds no `ScopeFixturePage` chunk, no `/__fixtures/` string and no flag list; an `a11y` build of the same tree has all three, which shows the search works. `npm run build` and a grep of `dist` agree. No switch is added or changed, so no flag comment changes beyond the header note in `src/lib/features.ts` that explains the `a11y` override.
- **The colour and font baseline.** `test/a11y/baseline.ts` writes `test/a11y/baseline.json`, committed, on the `a11y-off` build (`A11Y_BASELINE=record` rewrites it; otherwise the run compares). It records the computed colour and font-family of the body, the first Button inside `<main>`, a table header and a `.font-mono` element, each colour also as its painted sRGB pixel with a tolerance of 2, on `/`, `/tournaments`, `/membership` and `/tournaments/fx-t-done`. The first three pages have no table header or monospace element today, so those are recorded as not found there; the fourth page supplies them. Later steps must leave these values alone with every switch off.
- **The known-violations baseline.** `test/a11y/smoke.spec.ts` loads `/` on both builds and runs axe with the WCAG 2.0, 2.1 and 2.2 A and AA tags. `test/a11y/known-violations.json` lists each rule and selector found today, by build; a violation not in the list fails the test, a listed one is reported and attached, and a listed one that does not appear on a run is reported as not found, not as a failure. A script error or a request outside 127.0.0.1 also fails it. Today: `color-contrast` on three muted grey date and place lines (upcoming events and clubs lists) and `target-size` (a WCAG 2.2 rule) on the hero slideshow's dots. The hero advances every 5 seconds, so the current dot shows as `.w-5` and the others by label; all six selector forms are listed. Step 32 empties the list.
- **What else the suite checks.** `test/a11y/builds.spec.ts`: each port serves its own build and the switches in the page match it; the fixture page draws every name, rejects an unknown one and keeps the weekday, 7:00 PM, US Chess and ½ rules. `test/a11y/seeded-data.spec.ts`: the real handlers on the local database return the seeded prices, legacy string sections, round times, the champion note, the pinned post, documents and seats, and six protected endpoints answer 401 or 403 with no data for no token, a forged token, the fake service key and Basic auth, each in under 5 seconds; the Facebook endpoint answers 502 "not configured". `test/a11y/axe-detects.spec.ts`: axe finds known faults, reports none on a clean page and flags `target-size`. Mutation checks (a changed baseline pixel, a changed font family and a changed known-violation entry) each failed with a clear message before the files were restored byte for byte.
- **Checkpoint D opens here.** Steps 20 to 32 build the design system, and this is the browser layer they are tested against.
- Checks: `npm run lint` is 34 errors and 13 warnings before and after, none in a touched file (without the new ignores in `eslint.config.js` it reads 63 errors, from Wrangler's leftover bundles in `.wrangler/tmp`). `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run check:bundle` (no zod in 115 files) pass. `npm run test:all` passes: unit 1874 tests in 80 files (1601 in 74 before: 272 in the six new files and 1 in `app-routes.test.tsx`), integration 790 tests in 58 files (unchanged). `npm run test:a11y`, with `PW_CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`: 73 passed in 40.4 s, from a clean state, with both builds, the migrations, the seed and the baseline comparison; axe finds 8 known violations (3 `color-contrast` on `text-muted-foreground`, 5 `target-size` on the dots) and 0 new on each build. Wrangler logs "Network connection lost" while the fake sign-in service refuses a connection; the requests answer 401, as expected. `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/`, `src/lib/scanner` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed. The CI `a11y` job has not run on GitHub yet; its first pull request shows whether the Chromium that CI installs paints the same colours as the baseline recorded here.

### Step 21: tokens, dark mode, focus ring and motion

The second step of checkpoint D. It adds the token layer every later design step builds on, all behind `newLook`: with the switch off the site draws exactly what it drew before, and `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unchanged. The base was `27824cf` (step 20); nothing is committed yet.

- **Files.**
  - Packages: `@fontsource-variable/geist-mono` 5.3.0 and `@fontsource/instrument-serif` 5.3.0, both published Sunday, July 19, 2026, exact-pinned, with `package-lock.json` updated by `npm install`.
  - `src/index.css`, `src/main.tsx`, `vite.config.ts`, `src/components/ui/button.tsx`, `src/components/layout/Footer.tsx`, and comments in `src/components/ui/badge.tsx`, `src/lib/brand.ts` and `src/lib/features.ts`. New: `src/lib/colorScheme.ts`, `src/hooks/useColorScheme.ts`, `src/components/AppearanceControl.tsx`.
  - Tests: `test/unit/tokens.test.ts` (25), `test/unit/contrast.test.ts` (150), `test/unit/prepaint-gate.test.ts` (31), `test/unit/token-utilities.test.ts` (25), `test/unit/tokens-scopes.test.ts` (9), `test/unit/geist-mono-glyphs.test.ts` (4), `test/unit/appearance-control.test.tsx` (8), `test/unit/appearance-control-off.test.tsx` (4), `test/unit/redesign-status-step21.test.ts` (11), the shared stylesheet reader `test/unit/helpers/cssTokens.ts`, and `test/a11y/tokens.spec.ts` (18 tests that run on both builds; each skips on the build it does not check).
  - `index.html` is unchanged: its two `theme-color` metas already give `#1a2744` for light and `#0b1220` for dark.
- **Today's values on the plain `:root`.** The semantic tokens are `--ground`, `--surface`, `--surface-2`, `--raised`, `--ink`, `--ink-muted`, `--hairline`, `--field` (the field border), `--ok`, `--bad`, `--gold`, `--gold-ink`, `--gold-on-dark`, `--focus` and `--font-mono-face`, plus a text colour and a tint for each status (open, soon, full, waitlist, closed, live, partner, lca). The plain `:root` gives each one the value the site shows today: `--ground` is the stock `--background`, `--ink` is navy `#1a2744`, `--surface` is `#fff`, `--gold-ink` is `#c8a94a`, the statuses are the light tones `StatusBadge` draws, `--focus` is `color-mix(in oklab, var(--ring) 50%, transparent)`, which is the very value Tailwind compiles `ring-ring/50` to, and `--font-mono-face` is Tailwind's own monospace stack. The plain `:root` block stays first and the stock `.dark` block stays after it, untouched, so the baseline checks in `browser-test-wiring.test.ts` read the same values.
- **The new look.** Every new rule starts with `:root[data-look="new"]`, which `src/main.tsx` sets on `<html>` when `newLook` is on. Base is Looks-3 Minimal (navy ink, `#fff` and `#f7f8fa` surfaces, `#e5e7ed` borders, `#5b6478` muted, ok `#047857`, bad `#c0362c`, gold `#c8a94a` as a fill, gold-ink `#866a1e`, `--font-mono-face` `'Geist Mono Variable'` and then the system stack). `.dark` under the new look is the Looks-3 dark palette (`#0b1220`, `#111a2c`, `#1a2744`, ink `#e8ecf4`, muted `#9aa6bf`). `[data-theme="live"]`, `[data-theme="scholastic"]` and `[data-theme="heritage"]` carry the Looks-7, Looks-6 and Looks-1 tile values (heritage `--gold-ink` `#705718`; heritage under `.dark` keeps the Minimal dark grounds), and `[data-theme=""]` puts the base look back, light or dark. The shadcn variables (`--background`, `--foreground`, `--card`, `--muted-foreground`, `--border`, `--input`, `--ring` and the rest) point at the tokens only under the new look, on `<html>` and on every scope, so a component inside a scope takes that scope's colours and the stock `.dark` greys never show. `@theme inline` adds the utilities (`text-ink`, `text-ink-muted`, `bg-ground`, `bg-surface`, `bg-surface-2`, `bg-raised`, `border-hairline`, `border-field`, `bg-gold`, `text-gold-ink`, `text-gold-on-dark`, `text-ok`, `text-bad`, `ring-focus`, `text-status-open`, `bg-status-open-tint` and so on) and `--font-mono: var(--font-mono-face)`. The dark variant is `(&:where(.dark, .dark *))`.
- **Focus.** The Button's glow reads `ring-focus`, which with `newLook` off is today's half-strength ring, measured the same before and after (`oklab(0.708 0 0 / 0.5) 0px 0px 0px 3px`, border `oklch(0.708 0 0)`, no outline). Under the new look one rule outside the layers gives every `:focus-visible` element a 2px solid `--focus` outline with a 2px gap and turns off the shadcn glow while it shows, so a control draws one ring: navy on light grounds, gold on dark and live grounds, and gold inside a `bg-lca-navy` band (today's header, footer and the `PageHero` bands) or inside any element marked `data-ground="dark"`. The homepage hero is not a navy band: its navy is a tint laid over the photo beside the buttons, not around them, so until a later step marks it, its buttons, arrows and dots get the navy ring on a dark photo (about 1.2:1). The red and blue announcement banners are in the same position. Both are under Follow-ups, and `newLook` stays off until they are fixed.
- **Motion.** Under the new look, `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` sets every animation and transition to 0 seconds with no delay and one iteration. A zero-length animation still ends, so the dialogs that wait for their closing animation still close.
- **Fonts.** Geist Mono is imported once and named only in the new look's `--font-mono-face`, so with `newLook` off no page asks for it (the browser test watches the network on the finished event's page) and the eighteen `font-mono` uses keep the device's font. Instrument Serif 400 is `font-accent`; nothing uses it yet, and a font file is fetched only when a page uses it.
- **Appearance.** `src/lib/colorScheme.ts` holds System, Light and Dark, the `lca-appearance` key in `localStorage` (every read and write in `try`/`catch`; System removes the key), and `PREPAINT_SCRIPT`, a one-line ES5 script that sets `data-look="new"` and `.dark` before the first paint. `vite.config.ts` writes that script at the top of the page head only when the build has `newLook` on; it reads the switch from the text of `src/lib/features.ts` and, in the `a11y` mode only, from `VITE_FLAGS`, the same rule as `applyFlagOverrides`, because the config cannot import a file that reads `import.meta.env`. A build without the switch carries no script, so nothing can add `.dark` and wake the stock dark block. `src/hooks/useColorScheme.ts` applies a choice and follows the device setting as it changes, and adds `.dark` only when `newLook` is on. `AppearanceControl` (three buttons with `aria-pressed`, labelled "Appearance") sits in the current footer's bottom line under `newLook`; with the switch off the footer keeps its empty line.
- **The switches keep their own chunk.** `src/main.tsx` now imports `FEATURES`, which would fold the switches into the entry chunk; `builds.spec.ts` loads `features-[hash].js` to prove which build a port serves. A `codeSplitting` group in `vite.config.ts` keeps that file; the build's other 104 scripts are the same list as before.
- Checks: `npm run lint` is 34 errors and 13 warnings before and after, none in a touched file. `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run check:bundle` pass (no zod in 125 files; 115 before, plus the ten new font files). `npm run test:all` passes: unit 2141 tests in 89 files (1874 in 80 before; 267 in the nine new files), integration 790 in 58 (unchanged). `npm run test:a11y`, with `PW_CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`: 91 passed and 18 skipped (each `tokens.spec.ts` test on the build it does not check) in 1.2 minutes, from a clean state; "Baseline matches: no colour or font changed."; axe on `/` finds 8 known and 0 new on `a11y-off`, and 5 known and 0 new on `a11y-on`, where the three muted grey lines now pass under the new muted colour. `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/`, `src/lib/scanner` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed.

### Step 22: ThemeScope, the route-to-look map and the live fonts

The third step of checkpoint D. It adds the mechanism every themed area will use, and nothing renders it outside the browser-test fixture page, so the site draws exactly what it drew before and `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unchanged. The base was `aaa001b`; nothing is committed yet.

- **Files.**
  - Packages: `@fontsource/barlow` and `@fontsource/barlow-condensed` 5.3.0, both published Sunday, July 19, 2026, exact-pinned, with `package-lock.json` updated by `npm install --save-exact`.
  - New: `src/components/ThemeScope.tsx`, `src/lib/themeScopes.ts`, `src/styles/themes/live.css` (the `src/styles` folder is new). Changed: `src/pages/fixtures/ScopeFixturePage.tsx`.
  - Tests: `test/unit/themeScopes.test.ts` (18), `test/unit/theme-scope.test.tsx` (31), `test/unit/theme-scope-edges.test.tsx` (12), `test/unit/theme-scope-wiring.test.ts` (20), `test/unit/themeScopes-edges.test.ts` (14), `test/a11y/scope-switch.spec.ts` (4 tests on both builds) and `test/a11y/scope-switch-edges.spec.ts` (6 tests on both builds).
- **ThemeScope.** It takes `scope` (`base`, `heritage`, `live` or `scholastic`) and writes `data-theme` on a wrapper, `""` for base, which puts the base look back. The wrapper is `display: contents` so layout does not change; `layout="box"` keeps a real box (the fixture container needs one, because `tokens.spec.ts` reads its background), and `as` is `div`, `main` or `section`. Ordinary HTML attributes pass through, but a `data-theme` among them cannot override the scope. The token blocks are already in `src/index.css` from step 21, so a switch never shows base colours for a moment.
- **Nesting.** One level only. Base inside heritage is the one nesting allowed (editors and forms on a heritage page stay base); heritage inside live, every other pair and two levels deep are reported with `console.warn`, once per change. The rule lives in `nestingProblem` in `src/lib/themeScopes.ts`, because `react-refresh` objects to a component file that exports other things. The check runs in every mode except the production build.
- **routeScope(pathname).** It ignores case, a trailing slash, a query and a hash, as React Router does. The admin, workspace, manage, dashboard, account and board inbox prefixes are checked first and are always base; the six heritage pages (`/about`, `/governance/board`, `/governance/bylaws`, `/governance/minutes`, `/meeting`, `/champions`) resolve to heritage; everything else, the three redirect addresses included, is base. It returns only base or heritage: the live look follows event state and scholastic waits for WS11.
- **Fonts.** A scope's font CSS is imported lazily the first time that scope renders, and an import that fails is retried by the next one. Only live has a loader today; heritage arrives in step 30 and scholastic in WS11. `live.css` holds only `@import` lines: Barlow Condensed 500, 600, 700 and 800 and Barlow 400, 500 and 600, the weights the Looks-7 tile names. It binds no `font-family` yet, so no font file downloads; the production build carries no ThemeScope, no `live-*.css` and no Barlow files.
- **The fixture page.** Its container renders through `ThemeScope` with `layout="box"`. The scholastic fixture gains the grade-band tiles built from the `--*-tile` and hue tokens: K-3 Coral, K-5 Mint, K-8 Sky, K-12 Grape and a Gold tile marked "Tiles", the board's word for it, in the board's dark gold `#705718` read from `--status-soon` (the en dash is written as an escape because `browser-test-wiring.test.ts` bans dash characters in that file). Its header comment is rewritten. No control was added: the switch in AC8 is made by the test.
- Checks: `npm run lint` is 34 errors and 13 warnings before and after, none in a touched file. `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run check:bundle` pass (no zod in 125 files). `npm run test:all` passes: unit 2236 tests in 94 files (2141 in 89 before; 95 added in the five new files), integration 790 in 58 (unchanged). In the first full run `test/unit/drizzle-baseline.test.ts` timed out twice at 5000 ms while Playwright and wrangler ran at the same time; that file alone passed 26 of 26 and the later full run passed. `npm run test:a11y`, with `PW_CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`: 111 passed and 18 skipped in 1.8 minutes (91 and 18 before; 20 added: the two new specs on both builds); the baseline comparison matched and `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unmodified. The `Network connection lost` lines in the log come from the `/api/me` role-safety case in `seeded-data.spec.ts`, which calls the fake Supabase on a closed port. `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed.

### Step 23: the pages' ink and grounds on the tokens

The fourth step of checkpoint D, and the first half of making the existing pages follow dark mode and the looks (step 24 is the second: the status colours, the grey and slate classes and raw hex colours). The pages' navy text, white grounds and navy bands now use the token utilities, through a script and a reviewed map in which every entry gives its reason. With `newLook` off every class the map writes paints exactly the colour it replaced, so nothing visible changes and `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unchanged; only in a browser that cannot mix colours do two token forms with an opacity modifier fall back to full strength, which raises their contrast (see Deviations). The base was `bb8c8a6` (step 22); nothing is committed yet.

- **Files.**
  - New: `scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.ts` and its map `scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.map.json` (the `scripts/codemods` folder is new).
  - New unit tests, nine files with 189 tests: `test/unit/semantic-map.test.ts` (34: the proof of identity, and the tree held to the map), `test/unit/semantic-map-cascade.test.ts` (46: whole elements, through variants, `tailwind-merge` and CSS order), `test/unit/semantic-map-grounds.test.ts` (22: the grounds under the classes the map writes and keeps), `test/unit/semantic-colours-edges.test.ts` (33: the script's edges), `test/unit/announcement-banner-ground.test.tsx` (8: the banner's markers), `test/unit/moved-modules.test.tsx` (14), `test/unit/posts-panel-load.test.tsx` (7) and `test/unit/scoresheet-photos.test.tsx` (4), which hold what the lint fixes must keep, and `test/unit/redesign-status-step23.test.ts` (21: these records). With them, `test/unit/helpers/jsxClasses.ts` reads the class names and the element tree of a TSX file with the TypeScript parser for the cascade and grounds tests.
  - New browser specs, on both builds: `test/a11y/dark-spot.spec.ts` (3 tests) and `test/a11y/dark-grounds.spec.ts` (5). All but the `/support` check in `dark-grounds.spec.ts` skip on `a11y-off`.
  - Changed: `src/index.css` (`--color-brand-navy` in the brand `@theme` block, `.bg-brand-navy` in the rule that turns the focus ring gold, and a rule after it that gives the controls of a menu drawn inside a navy band the look's own ring back), `test/unit/tokens.test.ts` (that rule's three selectors, and one test for the menu's rule), `test/a11y/tokens.spec.ts` (two tests: the homepage hero and a club hero give their links the gold ring, and the menu the state filter opens in the `/tournaments` hero gives its options the look's own ring), and 75 TSX files rewritten by the script.
  - By hand, beyond the script: the `data-ground="dark"` markers (see below), `dark:text-white` on the three navy Buttons of the support pages (see Judgement calls), and the lint fixes, which needed three new modules, `src/areas/clubs/clubMap.ts`, `src/areas/membership/usChess.ts` and `src/hooks/useViewOnly.ts`, re-exports in `src/areas/clubs/index.ts` and `src/areas/membership/index.ts`, and new import lines in their importers.
- **The script and the map.** The script reads every TSX file under `src` with the TypeScript parser. It rewrites classes in string literals and in the text of template literals, and in the quoted strings of commented-out code. In a comment it takes whatever lies between two matching quote marks on one line as a quoted string, so a line of prose with two apostrophes in it counts as one too, and a watched class between them would be rewritten, though the script's header says prose never is. The seven comment rewrites in this tree are all commented-out code (see Follow-ups). The map lists the classes it watches (`text-lca-navy`, `bg-white`, `bg-lca-navy`), six rules tried in order, three sites (one string in one file, where the rule would be wrong) and six keep entries, each with a reason. A rewrite keeps the class's variants (`hover:`, `md:`, `[&_a]:`), its opacity modifier and its `!` mark. It stops before writing anything on a watched class with no rule or keep entry, a class that runs into a `${}` substitution, a string with an escape, a site or keep entry that does not name exactly one string, or a keep entry that matches nothing. `--dry-run` prints the plan; `--check` fails when anything is left to rewrite, which is how the unit test holds the tree to the map. The run rewrote 487 classes in 75 files and kept 39; a second run rewrites none. Run on a copy of the step 22 tree, the final map gives exactly this tree's class changes; every other difference in those files is one of the changes by hand listed above.
  - `text-lca-navy`: 381 to none. 340 became `text-ink` and 41 `text-brand-navy` (the 40 strings where it sits with an opaque `bg-lca-gold`, and the trophy on the gold square of the home page's membership band). 7 of the 381 were in commented-out code in `NewsPage.tsx` and `AnnualMeetingPage.tsx`.
  - `bg-lca-navy`: 102 to none, all of them `bg-brand-navy` forms: 79 plain, 4 `hover:bg-brand-navy/90`, and 19 tints at the same opacity, the 17 faint ones (`/5`, `/7`, `/8`, `/10`, `/[0.03]`, `/[0.04]` and `/[0.06]`), the hero photo's `/55` veil and the Exit button's `hover:/10` on the gold impersonation banner.
  - `bg-white`: 43, of which 39 are kept (5 white mats and 34 translucent veils on navy); the manage page's tab bar and the club map's "Show all clubs" button became `bg-surface`, and the scanner's remove button `bg-surface/90` with `hover:bg-surface`.
- **The brand navy.** `--color-brand-navy` (`#1a2744`) sits in the brand `@theme` block beside `--color-lca-navy`, so there are still exactly two `@theme` blocks. `bg-brand-navy` and `text-brand-navy` read it directly, and no look, scope or dark block sets it, so a navy band stays navy and navy text on gold stays navy in every look. `text-ink` is the navy that turns light in dark mode. The rule that turns the focus ring gold names `.bg-brand-navy` as well as `.bg-lca-navy`, so the header, footer and page heroes keep the gold ring under the new look. A menu drawn inside a band is a ground of its own: a second rule, after the gold one and one class more specific, gives the controls of a `bg-popover` panel inside a navy band or a `data-ground="dark"` element the look's own ring back (see Judgement calls).
- **Dark grounds marked.** `data-ground="dark"` now sits on the homepage hero's outer element (it holds the buttons, arrows and slide dots; the navy veil is a sibling of them), on the `urgent` and `info` tones of `AnnouncementBanner` (the gold tone and an unknown tone get none, and the navy tone is a `bg-brand-navy` box), and on the heroes of `ClubDetailPage` and `AdminClubPage`, whose navy is an inline style. With `newLook` off no rule reads the attribute.
- **Judgement calls.** Every place where a mapped class sits on a coloured ground was reviewed, from lists made with the TypeScript parser of each class string, its element and the nearest ground around it, and the script's dry run. Those lists looked for the ground only in the element's own file, so at first the review missed a light menu that another component draws inside a navy band (the focus ring call below). K reviews these calls before `newLook` is turned on (see Follow-ups):
  - Navy text on an opaque gold fill becomes `text-brand-navy`: the gold button (31 strings carry its full class set, 12 of them local copies of `GOLD_BUTTON`), the gold pills and badges, the gold announcement tone and the impersonation banner. The trophy icon on the home page's gold square is a site, because the gold is on its parent. `GOLD_BUTTON` itself, in `src/lib/brand.ts`, keeps `text-lca-navy`: it is not a TSX file, and that class is the same navy, fixed in every look.
  - Navy text on a gold tint (38 places: 15 in the tint's own class string, such as "Most Popular" on `/membership`, and 23 inside a tinted element; from `bg-lca-gold/4` to `/25`) becomes `text-ink`: the tint lies over the ground, which is dark in dark mode.
  - Navy tints keep the brand navy at their opacity (`bg-brand-navy/5` and the rest) rather than becoming ink tints. For a browser that cannot mix colours (Safari 15.4 to 16.1, which reads the `oklch()` colours of the plain `:root`, and older ones such as Chrome 109) Tailwind gives a brand colour with an opacity modifier the worked-out tint the old classes had, but a token class only its token at full strength, so an ink tint would show there as a solid navy box behind navy text: the section chips on `/tournaments`, "Your registration" on the tournament page, the support replies, the officer initials and the view-only note. In dark mode a navy tint barely shows on the dark ground, while the text on it (`text-ink`) stays readable; step 24 moves the tints to the ink, with the navy borders beside them and a fallback of their own (see Follow-ups). The veil over the hero photo, which keeps the photo dark behind white words, and the Exit button's hover on the gold banner are tints that stay navy in every look anyway.
  - The gold focus ring rule, which now names `.bg-brand-navy`, reached a light menu inside a navy band. `FilterDropdown` draws its menu (`bg-popover`, white in the light look) inside its own element, and on `/tournaments` that element sits in the navy hero (`on="navy"` inside `PageHero`), so with `newLook` on each option took the gold ring on white, 2.28:1; it had done so with `.bg-lca-navy` since step 21. A rule after the gold one gives the controls of a `bg-popover` panel inside a navy band or a `data-ground="dark"` element the look's own ring back: navy on the white menu (14.81:1) and gold on the navy menu of dark mode (6.51:1). It applies only under the new look, so nothing changes with `newLook` off. `semantic-map-grounds.test.ts` now follows each component to the places it is drawn: it finds this menu, and it fails on any other light card that holds a control inside a navy band unless that rule names its ground. `tokens.spec.ts` measures the ring on the menu in light and dark mode.
  - Selected navy controls (44 filter pills, tabs and step buttons) become `bg-brand-navy` and keep white text at 14.81:1. In dark mode a selected navy pill is close to an unselected one on `#111a2c`; see Follow-ups.
  - Three shadcn Buttons get their navy fill from their className (`bg-brand-navy hover:bg-brand-navy/90`): "Create ticket" and "Send reply" on `/support` and "Send reply & mark answered" on the admin support screen. Their label is the default variant's `text-primary-foreground`, which under the new look is the ground's colour, `#0b1220` in dark mode, about 1.3:1 on the navy. Each gains `dark:text-white`. `.dark` is set only when `newLook` is on, so with it off the label stays the `#fafafa` it is today, and under the new look in light mode the label is already white. They were the only navy fills whose own text colour followed the look; `semantic-map-grounds.test.ts` now checks every navy fill, and `dark-grounds.spec.ts` reads the label on `/support` on both builds.
  - White mats stay white: the club logos on the clubs list, the club page hero, the workspace list and the club's admin page, and the quiet zone around the two-step sign-in QR code, which a phone reads dark on white. The 34 translucent white veils (`bg-white/5` to `/60` and their hover forms) all sit on navy or on the dark hero photo, like `text-white`.
  - The scanner's remove button over a page photo follows the surface: its icon is `text-foreground`, which turns light in dark mode. The "Show all clubs" button over the club map follows the surface and the ink too: the map stays light in dark mode, the button becomes a dark chip on it, and with its old classes its hover (`bg-muted`) would have put navy on a dark ground. "Get directions" stays navy.

That is right once step 24 moves those tints to status tokens; until then dark mode would show light text on a light strip, which nobody sees because `newLook` stays off. See Follow-ups.
  - The tournament page's "See pairings / results" button puts navy text on a 15 per cent gold tint over the navy hero, about 1.3:1 in today's look. It becomes `text-ink`, unchanged with `newLook` off and readable in dark mode; the failure in today's look is older than this step (see Follow-ups).
  - Commented-out code is rewritten too, so bringing it back brings current classes.

There is no `bg-gray-50` or `bg-lca-cream`, so the card and section grounds were all `bg-white`.
  - `border-lca-navy` (73 in 23 files) and `ring-lca-navy` (1) are not in this map; see Follow-ups.

About 600 bytes of the CSS growth are the utilities the pages now use, and 218 the focus ring rule for a menu inside a navy band. The rest is rules for classes no page uses, 1,629 bytes against 733 at step 22: Tailwind reads every file in the repository for class names, the codemod's fixtures and map must spell out the old names, and these records name others (see Follow-ups). `npm run check:bundle` finds no zod.

It fails on a colour-contrast finding in navy text or on a white ground, and on any colour-contrast finding no later step's class explains. Results: `/` none (the five slide dots fail `target-size`, as listed for the light look); `/tournaments` one, the "LCA" pill's `text-[#7a5c00]` at 1.84:1, raw hex left to step 24 or 25 (and the gold "State champions" link fails `link-in-text-block`, as in the light look); `/membership` none. Before this step, an axe run of the same kind on the step 22 build found 3, 2 and 17 failures in navy text on those pages. `test/a11y/dark-grounds.spec.ts` reads the computed colours of every visible element on the same pages with a dark device, and on `/` with Dark chosen in Appearance on a light device, because axe leaves text it cannot place on a ground as incomplete: the navy left is the navy on gold, at 6.51:1 (11 text and icon parts on `/` and on `/tournaments`, 6 on `/membership`), no element has a white ground but the mats behind images, the navy bands are still `#1a2744` and `text-ink` is `#e8ecf4`. It also opens the new ticket form on `/support`, where the navy "Create ticket" button's label is white on `a11y-on` and the label token, as before, on `a11y-off`. A one-off axe run on 22 pages in dark mode, outside the specs and made before the navy tints, the support Buttons and the menu ring took their final form, found no failure in navy text or on a white ground; what it found is raw hex text (`text-[#7a5c00]`, `text-[#8a6d1f]`, `text-[#1668d8]`, steps 24 and 25), two club colours set inline (see Follow-ups), and older findings in both looks (`nested-interactive` on `/clubs`).
- Checks: `npm run lint` is 16 errors and 13 warnings (34 and 13 before), none in a touched file. `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run check:bundle` pass (no zod in 127 files). `npm run test:all` passes: unit 2426 tests in 103 files (2236 in 94 before; the nine new files hold 189, and `tokens.test.ts` has one more), integration 790 in 58 (unchanged). `npm run test:a11y`, with `PW_CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`: 122 passed and 27 skipped (111 and 18 before; 10 added on each build: the three pages of `dark-spot.spec.ts`, the five tests of `dark-grounds.spec.ts`, and the hero ring and filter menu tests in `tokens.spec.ts`, of which only the `/support` check runs on `a11y-off`); "Baseline matches: no colour or font changed." and `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unmodified. One earlier run failed one test, `seeded-data.spec.ts` "a write with no token is refused as well", where `POST /api/admin/members` answered 500 while the local server logged `SQLITE_BUSY`; the next run passed it (see Follow-ups). `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed.

### Step 24: status colours, greys and raw hex on the tokens

The fifth step of checkpoint D, and the second half of making the existing pages follow dark mode and the looks (step 23 was the first). The pages' status colours (emerald, green, red, amber, yellow, blue and purple), their grey and slate classes, the navy lines, ring and faint tints step 23 left, and their raw hex colours now use token utilities, through the same script with a second reviewed map in which every entry gives its reason. With `newLook` off every class the map writes paints exactly the colour it replaced, so nothing visible changes and `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unchanged; in a browser that cannot mix colours a token class with an opacity modifier would have fallen back to its token at full strength, and a block of its own gives those browsers the old tint back. A ratchet keeps raw colours from coming back, and a printed page takes the light look. The base was `4e4ad3a` (step 23); nothing is committed yet.

- **Files.**
  - New: `scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.status.map.json` (the second map), `src/areas/events/gradeConfirm.ts` (the lint fix, below), `test/unit/raw-colours.test.ts` (61: the ratchet) and its allowlist `test/unit/fixtures/raw-colours-allowlist.json`, `test/unit/dark-spot-later.test.ts` (6: what the dark spot run still credits to a later step), `test/unit/grade-confirm.test.tsx` (17: what the lint fix must keep), `test/unit/redesign-status-step24.test.ts` (16: these records), the helpers `test/unit/helpers/tones.ts` (the tone tokens, read from the stylesheet) and `test/unit/helpers/tailwindRules.ts` (a compiled stylesheet's rules with the at-rules around each), and `test/a11y/print-look.spec.ts` (3 tests on both builds; each skips on the build it does not check).
  - Changed: `scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.ts` (the comment quote fix, hex classes in a map, keep entries for a whole file), `src/index.css` (51 tokens and their utilities, the tone shades under the new look, the block for browsers that cannot mix colours, and `@media screen` around the dark look, the live look and heritage in dark mode), and 49 TSX files rewritten by the script. By hand: `src/areas/events/components/GradeConfirm.tsx`, `src/areas/events/index.ts`, `src/areas/events/uschessUpload.ts`, the import lines of `FamilyRegistrationPanel.tsx` and `TournamentDetailPage.tsx`, one line of `ScannerPage.tsx`, and the club map's loading note in `src/areas/clubs/components/LCAMap.tsx` and `ClubLocationPicker.tsx` (see Judgement calls).
  - Tests changed: `test/unit/semantic-map.test.ts` (195, 34 before: the status map's proof of identity, its cascade checks and the tree held to it, and the classes changed by hand on the club map's paper), `test/unit/contrast.test.ts` (206, 150 before), `test/unit/tokens.test.ts` (53, 26 before), `test/unit/semantic-colours-edges.test.ts` (44, 33 before), `test/unit/semantic-map-cascade.test.ts` (32, 46 before, because the faint navy tints it checked moved to the ink and the status map's forms are checked in `semantic-map.test.ts`), `test/unit/semantic-map-grounds.test.ts` (25, 22 before: the light islands now sit on a tint that follows the look, and no words colour or veil that follows the look sits on a ground that stays light), `test/unit/themeScopes.test.ts` and `test/unit/helpers/cssTokens.ts` (the rules on screens only), `test/unit/redesign-status-step23.test.ts` (a later T row may follow T1), and `test/a11y/dark-spot.spec.ts` (the classes a later step still owns).
- **The script and the map.** A map can now name a hex class (`text-[#1668d8]`, `accent-[#1a2744]`) wherever it names a class to watch, rewrite or keep, though what a rule writes is still a plain utility; a keep entry can name a file without a string, keeping its class in every string of that file; and the most particular keep entry that fits wins: one string, then one file, then everywhere. In a comment a quote mark opens or closes a string only where no letter or digit stands on its outer side, so the apostrophes of "don't" and "it's" never make one (step 23's follow-up). The status map watches 65 classes and has 58 rules, five sites and 31 keep entries. The run rewrote 339 classes in 49 files and kept 33; a second run rewrites none, and `--check` passes with either map. Run on a copy of the step 23 tree, the final map gives exactly this tree's class changes; every other difference in those files is one of the changes by hand listed above.
  - Status colours: Tailwind palette classes from 273 in 37 files to 32 in 5, all of them keep entries: emerald 121 (109 rewritten), red 37 (31), blue 43 (36), amber 37 (all), green 11 (9), yellow 9 (all), purple 2 (both), grey 10 (8) and slate 3 (none). Each shade becomes a token of its tone, named for the tone and Tailwind's step: `text-emerald-700` is `text-ok-700` and `bg-amber-50` is `bg-warn-50`. Ok is emerald, with the US Chess lookup's green as `ok-green`; bad is red; warn is amber, with the support pages' yellow as `warn-yellow`; info is blue, with the audit log's purple as `info-purple`; quiet is grey. `text-emerald-800` (34) becomes `text-ok` and `text-red-600` (5) `text-bad`, which already are those colours with `newLook` off. The two Open pills (the agenda list and the facts list on the tournament page) are sites and become `text-status-open` on `bg-status-open-tint`.
  - Navy: `border-lca-navy` from 73 in 23 files to none. The 27 opaque lines that share their string with an opaque `bg-brand-navy` become `border-brand-navy`, the Exit button's line on the gold impersonation banner becomes `border-brand-navy/30` (a site), and the other 45, 6 opaque lines on a light ground and 39 tints, become `border-ink` at their opacity. `ring-lca-navy` (1) becomes `ring-ink`. The 17 faint tints become `bg-ink` at the same opacity (`/5` 5, `/7` 5, `/8` 2, `/10` 1, `/[0.03]` 1, `/[0.04]` 2, `/[0.06]` 1); the hero photo's veil (`/55`), the navy buttons' hover (`/90`) and the Exit button's hover on gold (`hover:bg-brand-navy/10`, a keep entry) stay navy.
  - Hex: from 51 classes in 30 files to 44 in 27. `accent-[#1a2744]` (3) becomes `accent-ink`, `text-[#1668d8]` (2) `text-facebook-link`, and the Scheduled tag's `text-[#1e4d7b]` and its 15 per cent tint of the same blue become `text-info-banner` and `bg-info-banner/15`. The 44 left are in the allowlist (see Judgement calls).
- **Tokens.** 51, each with a utility in the `@theme inline` block, so there are still exactly two `@theme` blocks. On the plain `:root` each is exactly the colour of the class it replaced, as Tailwind's `theme.css` writes it, which `tokens.test.ts` holds: 44 tone shades (`--ok-50` to `--ok-950`, `--ok-green-*`, `--bad-*`, `--warn-*`, `--warn-yellow-*`, `--info-*`, `--info-purple-*`, `--quiet-100` and `--quiet-600`, one for each shade a page used), the info banner's blue `--info-banner` (`#1e4d7b`), `--facebook-link` (`#1668d8`), the scanner's move marks `--mark-warn` and `--mark-ok` (amber-200 and emerald-200), and the check-in toggle's fill `--ok-fill` (emerald-600); and two for a light island, `--muted-on-light` and `--veil-on-light`, which are today's `--muted-foreground` and `--muted`, the words and veil of the club map's loading note. Under the new look one block, on `<html>` and on every scope, points each shade at its tone's colour in the Looks-3 status tags: the shades up to 300 (tints and light lines) at the tag's tint, the shades from 400 (words, icons and dots) at its words. Ok is the open tag (its words are `--ok`), bad the full tag (`--bad`), warn the soon tag, info and the banner's blue the partner tag, and quiet the closed tag; heritage restyles none of them. The other six take values of their own: the Facebook link `#1668d8` in the light looks and `#4599ff` in dark mode and the live look; the marks a highlighter on a light card (`#fee685`, and `#bcf7db`, emerald-200 lightened until the ok words reach 4.5:1 on it) and a quarter of the gold and of the ok green over the card in dark mode (`#3f3e34`, `#1c4745`) and in the live look (`#3d3a2f`, `#184543`); the check-in fill `#047857` in every look; and the light island's words and veil the light look's muted words and muted ground, `#5b6478` and `#f7f8fa`, in every look and in dark mode, since the ground under them stays light.
- **Browsers that cannot mix colours.** Tailwind gives a token class with an opacity modifier its token at full strength in a browser that cannot mix colours (Safari 15.4 to 16.1, Chrome 109), where the class it replaced fell back to its worked-out tint. An `@supports not (color: color-mix(in lab, red, red))` block in `src/index.css` gives each of the 15 token forms with a modifier that the map writes (the seven ink tints, seven `border-ink` forms, of which the two hover forms sit inside `@media (hover: hover)` as Tailwind's own do, and `bg-info-banner/15`) the `#rrggbbaa` tint its old class fell back to, in its own selector. `semantic-map.test.ts` checks each against the old class's compiled fallback, and that the block holds nothing else and beats no rival colour on the same element. It applies in either look, so those browsers keep the navy tints they draw today (see Decisions).
- **Printing.** The dark look, the live look and heritage in dark mode now sit inside `@media screen`, so a printed page takes the light look of its scope whatever the device's setting: paper is white, and a browser prints no background unless asked. Before, with `newLook` on, a director printing from a dark device got the print page's crosstable words in `#e8ecf4` (1.18:1 on white) and its muted words in `#9aa6bf` (2.45:1). With print settled, the rating and score beside each name on the pairing sheet take `text-quiet-600` like the other greys: `#9aa6bf` on the dark screen (7.65:1 on `#0b1220`, where `text-gray-600` read 2.48:1) and `#5b6478` on paper. With `newLook` off none of these rules applies. `tokens.test.ts` reads a printed page's tokens, and `test/a11y/print-look.spec.ts` prints the seeded finished event's sheets.
- **Judgement calls.** Every place where a class the map writes or keeps sits on a coloured ground was reviewed, from the script's dry run, the lists the TypeScript parser makes of each class string with its element and the grounds above it (the pairs `contrast.test.ts` now checks) and the allowlist. K reviews these calls, with step 23's, before `newLook` is turned on (see Follow-ups):

A status word sits only on its own tint, which `contrast.test.ts` holds for every `text-status` class; the map writes one only on the two Open pills.

The audit log's entry colours (green for something added or finished, red for a removal, blue for an edit or a message, purple for one kind more) and the support ticket badges (Open and New in blue, Answered in green, In progress in yellow, Resolved in grey) are categories, not statuses. Every badge keeps its words, so the colour only groups them; under the new look blue and purple both take the partner tag's navy, and yellow, like amber, the soon tag's gold.

The unchecked toggle's 50 per cent green hover line is kept as it is, a hint that shows on a light or a dark card (see Follow-ups).

The navy stays `#1a2744` in every look, as with the white veils step 23 kept, and a 20 per cent tint on navy keeps the fallback Tailwind works out for it.

Its gold tone's `text-[#7a5c00]` waits for step 25 with the other gold.

The three slate dots of the state filter and the legend on `/tournaments` are kept, because they key the inline slate-400 the list gives an external event; each sits beside its words, and WS04 rebuilds the filters.

The Exit button's line on the gold banner stays navy, like the words on the banner.

The note now takes `text-muted-on-light` and the veil `bg-veil-on-light/30`: today's muted words and muted ground with `newLook` off, the same rules and colours, and the light look's `#5b6478` and `#f7f8fa` in every look under it, 5.16:1 on the paper and 5.29:1 under the veil. ### Step 25: gold text off light grounds

The sixth step of checkpoint D. Brand gold `#c8a94a` reads 2.28:1 on white, so the brief keeps it as text on navy only, where it reads 6.51:1. Every `text-lca-gold` on a light ground now takes the gold-ink token, and the gold the pages spelled as hex colours takes three gold tokens of its own, so under the new look no gold text sits on a light ground at 2.28:1. With `newLook` off every class paints the colour it replaced, so nothing visible changes and `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unchanged. A guard keeps gold text off light grounds from here on. The base was `3d80a0e` (step 24); nothing is committed yet.

- **Files.**
  - New: `scripts/codemods/gold-ink.ts` (the script that reads each gold class's ground, and the reader the guard uses), `scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.gold.map.json` (the third map, for the gold hex text classes), `test/unit/goldText.test.ts` (129: the guard) and its allowlist `test/unit/fixtures/goldAllowlist.ts`, `test/unit/gold-ink.test.ts` (87: the script on planted trees and on the real one), `test/unit/news-pinned.test.tsx` (3), `test/unit/news-pinned-off.test.tsx` (3) and `test/unit/news-pinned-flag.test.tsx` (3: the News page drawn both ways), `test/unit/reminder-bell.test.tsx` (8: the reminder bell's name), `test/unit/gold-icons-words.test.ts` (7: every gold-ink mark beside words, and the State champions link as it is), and `test/unit/redesign-status-step25.test.ts` (18: these records).
  - Changed: `src/index.css` (three tokens and their utilities), `src/areas/clubs/clubColors.ts` (`clubTextColor`), `src/lib/brand.ts` (`GOLD_BUTTON`'s navy), and 42 TSX files rewritten by the two runs. By hand: `src/pages/NewsPage.tsx` (the pinned post's word), and `ClubDetailPage.tsx`, `ClubsPage.tsx` and `AdminClubPage.tsx` (a club's colour as words, through `clubTextColor`).
  - Tests changed: `test/unit/tokens.test.ts` (58, 53 before), `test/unit/themeScopes.test.ts` (21, 18 before), `test/unit/token-utilities.test.ts` (28, 25 before), `test/unit/contrast.test.ts` (239, 206 before), `test/unit/semantic-map.test.ts` (225, 195 before), `test/unit/semantic-colours-edges.test.ts` (46, 44 before), `test/unit/raw-colours.test.ts` (74, 61 before) and its allowlist `test/unit/fixtures/raw-colours-allowlist.json`, `test/unit/dark-spot-later.test.ts`, `test/unit/redesign-status-step24.test.ts` and `test/a11y/dark-spot.spec.ts`.
- **The script.** `scripts/codemods/gold-ink.ts` reads every TSX file under `src` with the parser of `scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.ts` and finds the element each `text-lca-gold` sits on: the one whose className holds the string, directly or through a constant of the same file. From there up through the ancestors the first ground decides: navy for an opaque `bg-brand-navy` or `bg-lca-navy`, an inline navy, a hex ground on which brand gold reads 4.5:1 or anything passed to `PageHero`, `Navbar` or `Footer`; light for any other opaque ground (a hex one only where the light look's dark gold reads 4.5:1), a Card, an outline Button or what a dialog draws, unless the Card's or the dialog's className gives a ground of its own, which is read first. A tint lets the walk go on (an inline one too: a club colour's tint, an rgba below 1, or a colour given two hex digits of alpha, as the admin's colour preview draws it), a hover ground is not the resting ground, and a ground in a className counts only where its `?:` branch or `&&` guard also holds for the gold (the admin sidebar's icon is gold only in the branch where its link is navy). A component of the site among the ancestors is read inside, for the ground it paints where what it is given lands: around its `{children}` or the prop the gold is passed in, or, for a gold class given as its className, at the element that takes it; the caller's own classes land on each of its elements that takes the className or spreads the props. At the top of a component the walk follows the component to every place it is drawn, in its own file or in a file that imports it under any name (renamed, a default, a namespace or `lazy()`), `forwardRef()` and `memo()` included, but never a component of the same name from another module or a package. That is how the More menu's button, `NavLinkItem` and `DrawerLink` in `Navbar.tsx` are placed on the navy header, the scanner's panel is read as the card it paints, and each page is followed to its route in `src/App.tsx`. Anything it cannot settle (a string no className reads, a ground that may or may not apply, a ground it does not know, a component drawn on both kinds of ground or drawing what it is given on both) is a person's decision and an entry of `test/unit/fixtures/goldAllowlist.ts`; an entry for a use it can place is refused. It prints a review table of every use, with its ground, what decided it, what the run does and the element it sits on, and writes it to the file `--table` names (the scratchpad, not the repository). `data-ground="dark"`, the focus ring's marker, is not a colour: beside a navy of its own (the club heroes' inline navy) the navy decides, and on the home page hero, the one element marked with it and no navy, a darkened photo, the script cannot tell what brand gold reads, so a use there is unplaced, as on a hex ground where neither gold reads 4.5:1 (see Judgement calls). `--check` fails on any `text-lca-gold` left on a light ground, any `text-gold-ink` on navy or on a ground it cannot place, and any allowlist count that is wrong.
  - The run: `text-lca-gold` 135 in 43 TSX files (118 plain, 16 `hover:` and one `/90`). 82 became `text-gold-ink` in 32 files: the 80 on light grounds and the 2 in commented-out code (`AnnualMeetingPage.tsx` and `NewsPage.tsx`, whose comments sit on the page), so bringing that code back brings the current class. 51 are kept on navy or a dark ground, in 20 files: 32 by a navy fill on the element or an ancestor, 7 inside `PageHero`, 6 by the inline navy of the club heroes and 6 by the component rule. 2 are kept by the allowlist: StatusBadge's gold tone on its navy surface (line 28) and the tournament page's Upcoming tag in its navy hero (line 38), each in an object of strings no reading of the JSX can follow. A second run rewrites none, and `--check` passes. No person has yet reviewed every changed line against the review table: the 116 lines the two runs changed (one class to a line, in 42 files) and the hand changes are K's to review before `newLook` is turned on, and the table, which was written outside the repository, is made again from step 24's tree (see Follow-ups, "K reviews the step 25 judgement calls").
  - The gold hex text classes need no ground, since one token serves each wherever it sits, so they move by `scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.ts` with `--map scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.gold.map.json`, as the step 23 decision says a colour move does. The map watches three classes and has three rules, no site and no keep entry. The run rewrote 34 classes in 20 files (28 to `text-gold-ink-badge`, the add-seat pill's hover among them, 5 to `text-gold-on-dark-badge` and 1 to `text-gold-ink-badge-club`); a second run rewrites none.
- **Tokens.** Three, each with a utility in the `@theme inline` block, so there are still exactly two `@theme` blocks, and each on the plain `:root` at the hex colour the class it replaced spelled, which `tokens.test.ts` holds. `--gold-ink-badge` is `#7a5c00` there, the dark gold words of badges, pills and notes on a gold tint of 5 to 20 per cent and of four labels and counts; under the new look it is the Looks-3 soon tag's words, `#705718`, in base, scholastic and (passed on) heritage, and brand gold in dark mode and the live look. `--gold-ink-badge-club` is `#8a6d1f` there, a club page's Upcoming badge, and the same as `--gold-ink-badge` under the new look. `--gold-on-dark-badge` is `#f0d07a`, the light gold of a pill on a ground that stays dark, and only the plain `:root` sets it, so it is the same in every look. The new look sets the badge tokens in each block that sets `--gold-ink` but heritage's: base, dark and live (both on screens only) and scholastic, since scholastic and live keep their own whatever the device says. `--gold-ink` itself is unchanged: brand gold on the plain `:root`, the very colour `text-lca-gold` paints.
- **A club's colour as words.** An officer's title, a news date and the contact link on a club page, and a club's initials on `/clubs`, are drawn in the club's colour, and so is the club name in the admin's colour preview. The database gives every club brand gold until it picks a colour (`DEFAULT '#c8a94a'`, migration 0009), so most of them were brand gold words on white. `clubTextColor` in `src/areas/clubs/clubColors.ts` gives `var(--gold-ink)` for brand gold or no colour, which is brand gold with `newLook` off, and a colour the club chose as given. The five places use it; a club's own colour stays user data.

The tournament page's Bell and BellOff (lines 1062 and 1064) carry no gold class and the same words. Nothing was added.
  - Nothing relies on colour alone: every gold icon sits beside words in another colour, the pin has its word under the new look and the bell its words. What is left is the gold "State champions" link in the `/tournaments` hero, set apart from the words around it by colour until hovered (see Follow-ups).
- **Judgement calls.** Each from the review table, the dry runs and the pairs `contrast.test.ts` now reads. K reviews these calls, with step 23's and step 24's, before `newLook` is turned on (see Follow-ups):

They take `text-gold-ink-badge`, `#705718` under the new look, 4.70:1 or more on a tint up to 25 per cent over any light ground of the looks, and brand gold on the dark ones, 4.52:1 or more on a tint up to 20 per cent, the most these classes sit on.
  - `--status-soon` is `#7a5c00` on the plain `:root` too, but a status word sits only on its own tint (step 24's decision, which `contrast.test.ts` holds), and the pages' tints are `bg-lca-gold` at 8 to 20 per cent, not `--status-soon-tint`; so the dark gold words take a token of their own, and the tints stay as they are.

On a 15 or 20 per cent tint over the navy it reads 7.63:1 and 6.87:1, where brand gold would read 5.02:1 and 4.52:1; over the home page hero's darkened photo it reads 4.76:1 at the lightest the photo gets (a white pixel through the hero's filter, its 55 per cent navy and the gradient's 10 per cent), where brand gold falls to 3.14:1. `contrast.test.ts` works the photo out the same way.

  - StatusBadge is step 26's file: only its light gold tone's words change (line 35, to `text-gold-ink-badge`); its gold tone on the navy surface (line 28) stays `text-lca-gold` on navy, by the allowlist, and its other tones and their keep entries are untouched.
  - The hover forms and the one `/90` (the header's "Association" line) are all on navy and stay. None of the 135 sat on an opaque gold fill.
  - A ground counts as dark only where brand gold reads 4.5:1 on it. A hex ground stays put in every look, so it is navy only where brand gold reads 4.5:1 (a relative luminance of about 0.0526 or less) and light only where the light look's dark gold `#866a1e` does (about 0.871 or more); between them neither gold reads as words, as on the urgent banner's red (brand gold 3.58:1), the info banner's blue (3.84:1) and Facebook's blue (1.86:1), and a person decides. `data-ground="dark"` marks a ground for the focus ring and says nothing of its colour: the home page hero carries it over a darkened photo, where brand gold reads 4.29:1 at the lightest and 3.14:1 on a gold tint, so a gold use there needs an allowlist entry with the ratio it reads, and `contrast.test.ts` measures what the hero holds over the photo rather than the navy. `text-gold-ink` on a ground the reading cannot place is a problem that no entry can take. No `text-lca-gold` or `text-gold-ink` on the tree changes ground: the six on the club heroes are placed by their inline navy, and none sits on the hero, a banner or a hex ground. The hero's pill, `text-gold-on-dark-badge`, is now measured over the photo, at 4.76:1, rather than over the navy.

Those three are WS02's parts, `WS02_GOLD` in the fixture, named by the files WS02's Frontend list gives (`AboutMenu.tsx`, `MobileMenu.tsx`, `BottomTabs.tsx`); none exists yet, so each matches nothing, which the guard says, and a planted copy of each passes in its context and fails out of it. It holds the gold tokens of one kind of ground to it as well, placed by the same reading: a badge's dark gold (`text-gold-ink-badge` and `text-gold-ink-badge-club`, `#705718` in the light looks and about 2.2:1 on navy, and gold-ink as a variable in a class or a style or through `clubTextColor`) never on navy, and a pill's light gold (`text-gold-on-dark-badge`) only on a dark ground, wherever the reading finds the ground, inside a wrapper component that paints navy around what it is given or in a component drawn on navy under another name. The 12 uses it cannot place are entries of `GOLD_BADGE_ALLOWLIST` in the fixture: the tags of the audit log, the members table, the admin list of tournaments and the email campaigns and StatusBadge's light gold tone, each looked up by a status in an object of strings, the club page's Upcoming badge, and the home hero's pill over its darkened photo. A badge's entry names the code that reads its string (`meta.className` and the like), and the guard places every element whose className holds that code on the entry's ground, so moving one of those rows onto navy fails it. In a component told its ground by an `on` prop the branch for `on === 'navy'` is on navy and the other on a light ground, and every place such a component is drawn (`FilterDropdown`, `MultiSelectDropdown` and `StatusBadge`, eight places) must give the `on` its ground needs, as each does. Brand gold fails as an icon's paint too: a lucide icon's `color` (which lucide draws as the icon's stroke), `fill` or `stroke`, an SVG shape's, a style's `fill` or `stroke`, either token's variable in a style, and a colour a place passes into a component of the site, read where the component uses it. Every failure is planted in a temporary tree, with the class names put together when the test runs.
- **Lint.** 13 errors and 13 warnings before and after. No error is in a file this step touches: the errors are in `functions/` and `scanner/`. Of the 13 warnings, ten sit in eight files the step rewrites and stay as they were.
