The CSS adds the four utilities the pages now use (the three tokens' text classes and the add-seat pill's hover form) and the three tokens in the plain `:root` and the four new-look blocks, and drops the old hover rule nothing names any more; the old gold hex classes keep their rules because the gold map and the step 24 records spell them (see Follow-ups, "Classes only docs and tests name reach the CSS"). The tests and the script of this step put the class names they plant together when they run, so they add no rule. The JavaScript grows with the longer token names, the News page's word and `clubTextColor`. `npm run check:bundle` finds no zod.
- **The dark spot run.** `dark-spot.spec.ts` now credits a colour-contrast failure to a later step only for StatusBadge's light tones (step 26); gold text is credited to no step, so a gold text failure in dark mode fails it. Results, quoted from the run: "axe in dark mode on /: 25 rules passed, 0 colour-contrast failure(s), 1 other rule(s) with findings" (the five slide dots fail `target-size`, as listed for the light look); "axe in dark mode on /tournaments: 27 rules passed, 0 colour-contrast failure(s), 1 other rule(s) with findings": the LCA pill's words, `text-gold-ink-badge` on its gold tints, read 5.08:1 where `text-[#7a5c00]` read 1.84:1, and the one other finding is the gold "State champions" link's `link-in-text-block`, as at steps 23 and 24; "axe in dark mode on /membership: 25 rules passed, 0 colour-contrast failure(s), 0 other rule(s) with findings".
- Checks: `npm run lint` is 13 errors and 13 warnings before and after, no error in a touched file. `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run check:bundle` pass (no zod in 127 files). `npm run test:all` passes: unit 3117 tests in 115 files (2770 in 107 before; the eight new files hold 258 and the changed ones 89 more), integration 790 in 58 (unchanged). `npm run test:a11y`, with `PW_CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`: 125 passed and 30 skipped (unchanged: no test was added, and `dark-spot.spec.ts` now credits only StatusBadge's light tones to a later step); "Baseline matches: no colour or font changed." and `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unmodified. The last run logged no `SQLITE_BUSY`; earlier runs of the step logged it twice from a local server, about when `builds.spec.ts` started, and no test failed (see Follow-ups, "The a11y servers share one database (step 23)"). `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed.

### Step 26: status tags, the gold button, filter chips, date blocks and the scroll panel

The seventh step of checkpoint D. It builds the base components the later workstreams assemble pages from. StatusBadge gains a status prop that shows each of eight statuses in words, as the Looks-3 tile's tag on the status tokens, and its tone and on props keep drawing today's pills class for class; StatusDot says its state in words under the new look; the shared Button gains a gold variant; and `FilterChip`, `DateBlock` and `ScrollPanel` are new. The new components are drawn only on the fixture page, which exists only in the accessibility test build, and no page file changes; the one existing part whose output changes is StatusDot, whose words appear in the `/tournaments` list with `newLook` on. With `newLook` off nothing a visitor sees changes, so `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unchanged. The base was `a74f221` (step 25); nothing is committed yet.

- **Files.**
  - New: `src/lib/badgeStatus.ts` (the statuses and their default words), `src/lib/drawsNothing.ts` (whether a React node draws nothing, which StatusBadge asks of a status tag's words and ScrollPanel of its header), `src/components/ui/filter-chip.tsx`, `src/components/DateBlock.tsx` and `src/components/ScrollPanel.tsx`.
  - New tests: `test/unit/status-badge.test.tsx` (50), `test/unit/status-badge-edges.test.tsx` (31), `test/unit/status-dot-newlook.test.tsx` (9), `test/unit/status-dot-edges.test.tsx` (6), `test/unit/button-gold.test.tsx` (14), `test/unit/button-gold-edges.test.tsx` (80), `test/unit/filter-chip.test.tsx` (12), `test/unit/filter-chip-edges.test.tsx` (7), `test/unit/date-block.test.tsx` (20), `test/unit/date-block-edges.test.tsx` (38), `test/unit/scroll-panel.test.tsx` (9), `test/unit/scroll-panel-edges.test.tsx` (7), `test/unit/draws-nothing.test.tsx` (31), `test/unit/base-components-scope.test.ts` (9: the new parts are drawn on the fixture page only), `test/unit/base-components-types.test.ts` (37: what the components' types let a caller write, compiled with the site's settings), `test/unit/redesign-status-step26.test.ts` (24: these records), `test/a11y/components.spec.ts` (15 tests on each build; three skip on the build they do not check) and `test/a11y/components-edges.spec.ts` (23 tests on each build; five skip on `a11y-off`, which has no dark mode and no words beside the dot).
  - Changed: `src/components/StatusBadge.tsx`, `src/components/ui/button.tsx`, `src/lib/brand.ts` (a comment on `GOLD_BUTTON`), `src/index.css` (one rule for browsers that cannot mix colours), `src/pages/fixtures/ScopeFixturePage.tsx` and `scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.status.map.json` (StatusBadge's keep entries name their one string).
  - Tests changed: `test/unit/raw-colours.test.ts` (77, 74 before) and its allowlist `test/unit/fixtures/raw-colours-allowlist.json`, `test/unit/tokens.test.ts` (59, 58 before), `test/unit/semantic-map.test.ts`, `test/unit/contrast.test.ts`, `test/unit/goldText.test.ts`, `test/unit/dark-spot-later.test.ts`, `test/unit/browser-test-wiring.test.ts` (85, 83 before: the two new specs join its dash and wording check), `test/unit/redesign-status-step24.test.ts`, `test/unit/redesign-status-step25.test.ts`, `test/a11y/dark-spot.spec.ts`, `test/a11y/builds.spec.ts`, `test/a11y/scope-switch.spec.ts` and `test/a11y/scope-switch-edges.spec.ts`.
- **StatusBadge.** `<StatusBadge status="..." on="light">` is the status path, `<StatusBadge tone="..." on="...">` the tone path.
  - The status path draws the tile's tag (`.t-tag` on the Looks-3 Minimal tile): 24px high, a 6px radius, 12px words in weight 500 and a 6px gap, the same box in every look. Each status takes its pair of tokens, `bg-status-<status>-tint` and `text-status-<status>`, written in one string per status, so its words always sit on their own tint; the closed tag adds the tile's inset hairline. Its words are its children, or, when it has none or they draw nothing (nothing at all, a boolean, blank text, or a list or a fragment holding only those, as `drawsNothing` in `src/lib/drawsNothing.ts` tells them), its status's default words; an element of any other kind is taken as words, since what it draws is known only when it renders. The tag carries `data-status` and `data-slot="status-badge"`.
  - Each tag has the tile's mark before its words, in the words' colour and hidden from screen readers, so a status shows by shape as well as colour: a dot for open and live, a dashed ring for soon, a half-filled ring for full and waitlist, a bar for closed, and for partner the tile's external mark after its words. The LCA tag has no mark yet: the tile's is the rook, which step 28 builds as `LcaMark` (see Follow-ups).
  - The live tag's dot is static: no animation class, and an inline `animation: none`, so nothing that might animate a dot later reaches it.
  - The types: `StatusBadgeProps` is a union of the tone path (a tone and words, both required) and the status path (a status, `on="light"` and optional words), so a badge cannot take both or neither, and a tone pill cannot be written with null, false or undefined for its words; a status tag never shows its mark without words, since children that draw nothing give it its default words.
  - The tone path is unchanged: `TONE_CLASSES` keeps its ten strings on lines 26 to 41, where the gold guard and its allowlist name them, and the header comment above it was rewritten in one line fewer, which leaves room for the import of `drawsNothing`, to keep them there; `STATUS_CLASSES` holds its eight strings on lines 59 to 66, where `contrast.test.ts` finds them.
  - The statuses and their default words sit in `src/lib/badgeStatus.ts` (`BADGE_STATUSES`, `BadgeStatus` and `STATUS_LABELS`), since the linter's only-export-components rule is an error here and `StatusBadge.tsx` exports components and types only; it re-exports the type.
- **StatusDot.** It gains a `label` prop. Under the new look it draws its words beside the dot (the label, or its state's words when it has none or a blank one: Registration open, Opening soon, Registration closed, and Coming soon for a status it does not know, open and closed taking the status tags' words from `src/lib/badgeStatus.ts`) in the muted ink at 11px, the dot is hidden from screen readers and takes a token colour (`bg-ok-500`, the ok words colour, for open; brand gold for draft; the border colour for closed and the rest), and there is no title. With `newLook` off it is today's span, class and title, a closed registration's title still "Coming soon" as before, and a label that is not blank is its title. Its one caller, the `/tournaments` list, passes no label and draws it for each upcoming LCA event, whose `registration_status` is draft, open or closed (`NOT NULL` with a `CHECK` in `migrations/0005_email_registration_support.sql`, and an enum in the contract), so with `newLook` on its rows say Registration open, Opening soon or Registration closed. HomePage keeps its own copy (see Follow-ups).
- **The gold button.** `<Button variant="gold">` draws `GOLD_BUTTON`'s classes, which `button.tsx` imports from `src/lib/brand.ts`: the brand gold fill and navy words (6.51:1) in weight 600, which stay as they are in every look and in dark mode, never white on gold. `GOLD_BUTTON` stays the quoted literal it was, for its 18 importers, and the six variants the Button had keep their classes to the letter.
- **FilterChip** (`src/components/ui/filter-chip.tsx`). The tile's chip: 32px high, a 6px radius, 13px words in weight 500. A toggle with `aria-pressed` when given `onPressedChange`, a plain label otherwise; an optional count in the monospace face at 11px, in the muted ink while the chip is off and in the chip's words while it is on (the muted ink on the gold tint reads 4.08:1 with `newLook` off); and, with `onRemove`, a remove button named "Remove filter: <name>", 24 by 24 pixels, beside the toggle in a group named for the filter, never inside it. Off, the chip is the surface with a hairline and the ink; on, it is the soon tag's tint and words with a gold-ink line, as the tile draws it, and a check mark, so the state shows by shape too.
- **DateBlock** (`src/components/DateBlock.tsx`). The date tile of the decided boards (Tourn-List-Final, Home-Final): the weekday, the day and the month stacked in a 64px box, inside a `<time>`. Its words are `formatDate`'s from `src/lib/format.ts`, so every date carries its weekday ("Sat, Oct 24", with the year when asked), read once by a screen reader while the stacked parts are hidden from it. `dateTime` is the Central calendar day from `zonedParts`, imported by its own path, `@domain/format/date`, as `domain/events/eventMode.ts` imports it, so it is a valid date string for a bare date, a Louisiana wall-clock time and an instant alike, and always the day the words name. A value it cannot read shows as given, outside a `<time>`; no value shows nothing. It takes `stored: 'utc'` for a `datetime('now')` column, as `formatDate` does.
- **ScrollPanel** (`src/components/ScrollPanel.tsx`). From K's October 8 note: `ADMIN_SCROLL` made a component, a box bounded by the viewport (70vh, which a `max-h-*` in its className replaces) that scrolls on its own with its overscroll kept inside, framed by a hairline on the surface, with an optional header held at its top on a ground of its own; a header that draws nothing (false, blank text or an empty fragment, as `header={cond && ...}` gives when its condition fails) adds no header bar. It is a region named by its `label`, in the tab order (`tabIndex` 0), so Tab reaches it and the arrow, Page Up, Page Down, Home and End keys scroll it. Its header is drawn over the top of the scrolling box, so a browser takes a control under it for one already in view and leaves it hidden there when the keyboard moves to it, as Shift+Tab does after a person scrolls a form down (WCAG 2.4.11, Focus Not Obscured). The panel's scroll padding is therefore the header's height and the focus ring's 4px reach (the new look's 2px gap and 2px line, which also covers today's 3px glow), read as the header is drawn and again, through a `ResizeObserver`, whenever its height changes, so the browser scrolls such a control out from under the header, ring and all; with no header there is no padding. No page uses it yet (see Follow-ups).
- **Browsers that cannot mix colours.** On the plain `:root` the soon and LCA tints are 20 per cent of brand gold mixed with transparent, which a browser that cannot mix colours cannot read, so a soon or LCA tag, and a chip that is on, would lose its tint there with `newLook` off. The block for those browsers in `src/index.css` gains one rule of another kind: on `:root`, both tints as `#c8a94a33`, the fallback Tailwind writes for `bg-lca-gold/20`. The new look's values are plain hex colours on selectors that outrank it, so nothing else changes; `tokens.test.ts` holds it.
- **The fixture page.** After the Status section it draws the base components (`BaseComponents`, a component of the file): a tag for each of the eight statuses in its default words and two with words of their own ("Opens Mon, Oct 12", "Live · Round 3"), the three StatusDots, the gold Button ("Join the waitlist"), four filter chips that toggle (two with a count, one of them removable too) and an applied filter that can only be removed, three date blocks (a bare date, a wall-clock time and an instant, the last with its year) and a scroll panel of eleven setup steps under a header, each step a box to tick, so the keyboard has something to move to under the header. Its header comment was rewritten in fewer lines to make room for the imports, so the lines the gold guard pins (114 to 116) stay, and nothing on it is real data.
- **Judgement calls.** K reviews these calls, with those of steps 23 to 25, before `newLook` is turned on (see Follow-ups):
  - The light tones are kept, each in its one string: the green, red and blue light pills of the tone path keep their Tailwind colours rather than moving to the status tokens. The tone path keeps its ten strings to the letter, as the Button keeps its variants, so today's callers (the Scholastic page and the fixture's three pills) draw exactly what they drew; the pills are opaque light grounds with words of their own (6.68:1, 5.26:1 and 8.11:1), which read the same on any ground, dark mode included; and new code shows a status through the status prop. The status map's eight entries for StatusBadge's light tones and StatusDot's dot now name their one string, and their allowlist lines are gone, so the ratchet checks the file like any other.
  - The status path takes the tile's tag, not today's pill: the brief says the tile wins, and no page draws the status path yet. The tone path keeps the pill.
  - A status tag sits on the look's own ground only, which `on="light"` says and its type requires; on a navy band a status takes the tone path's navy surface. The gold guard reads `on` at every place StatusBadge is drawn, so a status tag without it would read as one on navy.
  - The default words, kept in one module: Registration open, Opens soon, Event full, Waitlist, Registration closed, Live, Partner event and LCA event. The boards name Registration open, Waitlist, Partner event and LCA event (the tile), Event full (Event-Final-States) and Live (the tile's "Live · Round 3", whose round a caller adds); Opens soon and Registration closed keep the fixture page's words, since the tile's "Opens Mon, Oct 12" needs a date and its "Closed" stands among other registration tags.
  - The marks follow the tile, but for the LCA tag's rook, which waits for `LcaMark`.
  - StatusDot's words are the muted ink at 11px, like the date line beside them in the `/tournaments` rows: 5.93:1 on white and 7.10:1 on the dark surface. The dot under the new look takes `bg-ok-500`, the open words colour, as HomePage's copy does.
  - A closed registration's dot says Registration closed under the new look, as the event page says "Registration is closed.", where the dot's title has said Coming soon since the dots were made one; now that its words are seen, they must not send a parent off to wait for a registration that is over. With `newLook` off its title stays Coming soon, since nothing may change there before the switch, and a title is read by few.
  - A status tag's words, and ScrollPanel's header, count as nothing only for what `drawsNothing` can see without drawing: nothing, a boolean, blank text, and lists and fragments of those. An element of any other kind, an empty `<span />` or a component that draws nothing included, is taken as words, rather than having StatusBadge draw its children to find out.
  - FilterChip: the soon pair with a gold-ink line and a check mark when it is on, as the tile draws it, rather than today's navy fill; the remove button at 24 by 24 pixels, the least WCAG 2.2 allows, inside the 32px chip; a chip that can only be removed is a label, not a toggle; and the list that draws a chip moves the focus after a removal, which the fixture page does not.
  - DateBlock is the boards' tile, with `formatDate`'s words for a screen reader, rather than the words alone: the decided boards draw a date in a list and in a hero as the tile. `zonedParts` is imported by its own path rather than added to the `domain/format` barrel, whose functions write text for people.
  - ScrollPanel draws its own frame and ground (a hairline, the surface), since its header needs an opaque ground to scroll under, and its header is a `div`, not a `<header>`.
  - ScrollPanel keeps a control the keyboard moves to clear of its header with a scroll padding of the header's height and 4px, and the header's height is read as it is drawn and whenever it changes, rather than written as a fixed class, as the pages' anchors are kept clear of the site header: a caller's header can be any height and wraps on a phone, and a padding shorter than the header would leave a control hidden under it. The padding is set on the element, so a change of the header's height draws nothing again, and a caller's className does not change it. The header stays sticky inside the scrolling box, as the brief's sticky header slot says, rather than standing above a box that scrolls on its own, which would hide nothing but would make the header no part of the region.
  - On `a11y-off` the two closed badges are kept out of the fixture page's axe run, with the reason in the spec and a test of their one finding, rather than listed in `known-violations.json`, which is never added to.
- **Lint.** 13 errors and 13 warnings before and after. No error is in a file this step touches: the errors are in `functions/` and `scanner/`, and the warnings are the fetch and import warnings and four hook warnings in files it does not touch.

Built from the site's own files only (without `docs/`, `test/`, `scripts/` and the Markdown records at the root), the CSS is 113,786 bytes before and 115,394 after (1,608 more): the utilities the status tags, StatusDot's words, the gold variant and the three new components use, and the fallback rule. The three components are drawn only on the fixture page, which the site's build leaves out, but Tailwind reads every file, so their classes are in its CSS too; the tests of this step put together the class names they plant when they run. The JavaScript grows with the status path, its marks and StatusDot's words, `drawsNothing`, and the Button reading `GOLD_BUTTON`. The fixes after review (a fragment's words, a closed registration's dot, a blank label, a header that draws nothing) add 214 bytes of JavaScript and no CSS: the stylesheet is the same file, byte for byte, as before them. The scroll padding added after the second review changes neither: `ScrollPanel` and the fixture page are in the accessibility test build only, and the fixture's boxes to tick use classes the stylesheet already had, so the CSS is the same file and the JavaScript the same 2,571,529 bytes in 104 files. `npm run check:bundle` finds no zod.
- **The browser runs.** `test/a11y/components.spec.ts`: on both builds every `[data-status]` element on the four fixtures has words and axe finds nothing ("22 rules passed, 0 with findings" for each fixture on each build; 21 before the scroll padding, whose inline style axe's `avoid-inline-spacing` now checks and passes), the closed badges' one finding on `a11y-off` is today's muted pair, the live dots are static with no reduced motion asked for, and the gold Button, the chips, the date blocks, the scroll panel and StatusDot do what this block says, the fixture's closed dot saying Registration closed on `a11y-on`; and with the scroll panel scrolled so its fourth step's box sits under the header, Shift+Tab from the fifth box moves to the fourth and the panel scrolls it out from under the header, where a point at its centre reaches it, on both builds. Built without the scroll padding, that test fails on both builds: the panel stays at 148 pixels down and the box stays hidden under the header, as review found. `test/a11y/components-edges.spec.ts`: the site's own axe rule finds words on all 13 `[data-status]` elements, and on `a11y-on` on the three dots, in every fixture, in light and dark mode, and fails the four it plants; two pictures of each live tag a second apart are the same, and a stylesheet that turns every status mark leaves the live dots still; the gold Button keeps navy words at 4.5:1 or more at rest and under the pointer, in dark mode too; the keyboard reaches each chip's toggle and remove button and then the panel, in order, each with a ring; the components fit a phone and axe finds nothing there; the dark heritage, scholastic and live fixtures pass axe; the fallback rule gives the soon and LCA tints `#c8a94a33` with `newLook` off and changes nothing under it; and StatusDot's words reach 4.5:1 on a selected row. `dark-spot.spec.ts` credits no class to a later step now, so any colour-contrast failure in dark mode fails it: "axe in dark mode on /: 25 rules passed, 0 colour-contrast failure(s), 1 other rule(s) with findings" (the five slide dots' `target-size`, as before); "axe in dark mode on /tournaments: 27 rules passed, 0 colour-contrast failure(s), 1 other rule(s) with findings" (the State champions link's `link-in-text-block`, as before), with StatusDot's words now in the list's rows; "axe in dark mode on /membership: 25 rules passed, 0 colour-contrast failure(s), 0 other rule(s) with findings".

### Step 27: chess data, result cells and the table primitives

The eighth step of checkpoint D. Chess data reads as the brief asks under the new look: scores and tiebreaks are written with ½ by `formatScore`, their figures are in the monospace face with tabular figures, each round of a crosstable shows its result by its letter, by a drawn mark for the colour played and in words, and the two tables' headers are real header cells, the player's name heading each row. The table primitives gain what that needs, each opt-in: `TableHead` says `scope="col"`, `TableRowHeader` is new, `stickyFirst` pins the first column or the first two on the opaque surface, `label` makes the table's box a named region in the tab order and `bounded` bounds it with its header held at the top. With `newLook` off both tables draw today's markup with `scope="col"` added to their header cells, which draws nothing new, so nothing a visitor sees changes and `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unchanged. The base was `a12c74e` (step 26); nothing is committed yet.

- **Files.**

- **ResultCell** (`src/components/ResultCell.tsx`). The result cell of the Live-C board (`.rc`): 24px high, a 6px radius, at least 58px wide, a 4px gap and 13px figures in the monospace face at weight 500, holding the result's letter, the mark of the colour the player had and the opponent's number, as "W ○ 17". The marks are inline SVG circles 8px across in the words' colour, a 1.5px ring for White and a filled disc for Black, drawn because the monospace face has neither character (see Verify items). The letter, the mark and the number are hidden from screen readers, and the cell's words, in a visually hidden span, say the game: "Won with White against 17", "Lost by forfeit against 7", "Full-point bye", "Half-point bye", "Did not play", "No result yet, with Black against 4"; an `aria-label` on a span with no role is not allowed in ARIA 1.2. A win, a loss and a draw take the ok, bad and closed tints, with words that reach 4.5:1 on them in every look (`text-ok-700` on `bg-ok-50`, `text-bad-700` on `bg-bad-50`, `text-ink` on `bg-quiet-100`); a forfeit, a bye, a round not played and a game with no result yet sit on the surface in a dashed outline in the field colour, in the muted words, as the board draws them. A mark shows only where a colour was played: a win, a loss, a draw and a game with no result yet. On paper the cell gives up its height and its 58px width and may wrap. `ResultLegend`, the key, says that each game shows the result, the color played and the opponent's number, then gives each letter with its words and the two marks with the words White and Black.
- **The table primitives** (`src/components/ui/table.tsx`). Each new prop is opt-in: a table drawn as the fixture page drew it gets today's markup to the letter but for `scope="col"` on its header cells, and its box keeps its three classes and gains no attribute.
  - `TableHead` writes `scope="col"` unless given another scope, which draws nothing new; `TableRowHeader` is a `th` with `scope="row"`, spaced like a cell, in weight 500.
  - `stickyFirst` pins the first column (`true`) or the first two (`2`) to the left edge while the table scrolls sideways. The table then draws its row lines on the cells (`border-separate`), so a line moves with a pinned cell rather than staying where a collapsed table draws it, and each pinned cell paints the opaque surface (`bg-surface`: `#fff` in the light looks, `#111a2c` in dark mode, `#0e1526` in the live look, `#fffdf8` in heritage) with its row's tint laid over it as an image. A row's tint is a custom property, `--row-tint`, which the row paints as well, so a pinned cell always matches its row: today's header tint and first place's gold tint are set that way, the hover tint and a selected row's tint by the table itself, and the rows do not fade, so a row and its pinned cells change together. The second column sticks at the first's width, read from the first cell as the table is drawn and again through a `ResizeObserver` when it changes, since a shared place such as 12-15 widens its column; a line down the last pinned column's right edge, in the border colour of the look the table sits in, marks where the rest scrolls under it.
  - `label` makes the box the table scrolls in a region named by it, in the tab order with the focus ring, as `ScrollPanel` is, so the keyboard can scroll a table wider than a phone. `bounded`, which the types allow only with a label, bounds the box by the viewport (70vh, which a `max-h-*` in `containerClassName` replaces) with its overscroll kept inside, and holds the header row at the top on the surface, its corner above the pinned columns. Both keep a control the keyboard moves to clear of what is pinned, with a scroll padding of the pinned header's height and the pinned columns' width plus the focus ring's 4px reach, measured as `ScrollPanel` measures its header (WCAG 2.4.11). On paper every cell is static, the pinned cells and a bounded box's header with them, and a bounded box prints whole; the print rule is written important, since the selectors that pin a cell or the header are more specific than it.
  - `containerClassName` carries the box's classes, which today's tables put on a scroll wrapper of their own. The file imports neither `ScrollPanel` nor `ADMIN_SCROLL`, whose importers `test/unit/base-components-scope.test.ts` pins; the bound is written in the file.
- **The two tables.** Under `newLook`, `Crosstable` and `StandingsTable` (`src/areas/live/components`) are drawn through the primitives: every figure (the pairing number, the rating, the points, W-D-L and the four tiebreaks) is in `font-mono`, the table keeps `tabular-nums`, scores and tiebreaks are written by `formatScore` (2½, 13½, ½), the number or the place and the player's name are pinned with the name the row's header, and the box is a region named for its section ("Open standings", "Open crosstable"). Each round of the crosstable is a `ResultCell`, so the w and b line and the cell's title are gone, and the key sits below it; a missing tiebreak is a plain hyphen with "none yet" for a screen reader where today's table draws an em dash. Today's header tint and first place's gold tint stay, the header cells keep their words, the place stays in the sans face as the board's Place is, and the footnote and the key are 12px, where today's lines are 11px. With `newLook` off each component draws what it drew, the function kept as it was but for `scope="col"` on its header cells (`TodaysCrosstable`, `TodaysStandingsTable`), and `cellFor` stays the one reader of the pairings.
- **The pages.** The print page's score going into the round is `formatScore`'s under `newLook` and its own formatter's without it, as today: that formatter writes ½ for a half but misprints a half score of 10 or more whose whole part ends in 0, 10.5 as 1½, 20.5 as 2½ and 100.5 as 10½ (see Follow-ups). The page's two `border-gray-300` row rules and its `text-quiet-600` words stay. The public pairings list writes a drawn game ½-½ under `newLook`, as the print page and the results entry write it, and the stored 1/2-1/2 without it; `formatScore` cannot, since a result is not a number, so a line in the page's own `formatResult` does. `ResultsEntry` says `scope="col"` on its four header cells under `newLook`; its halves were already ½. The manage page itself is not changed: it draws the two tables and the results entry, and its US Chess report keeps its own total (see Follow-ups). `TournamentDetailPage.tsx`, the page the baseline samples, is not changed either.
- **The fixture page.** At the end of `BaseComponents`, after the scroll panel, a sample crosstable of six made-up players after four of five rounds, the fifth paired with no results yet: every result code, a win, a loss and a draw with each colour, whole-number totals, first place's row tinted, in a `Table` with `stickyFirst={2}`, `bounded` and the label "Sample crosstable", 192 pixels high and 840 wide, so it scrolls both ways in every look, with its key. The header comment is one line shorter for the new import, so the lines the gold guard pins (114 to 116, 213, 217 and 218) stay, and the page still holds no en or em dash, no half point and no point; the 13 `[data-status]` elements, the five Buttons and the standings table's three header cells are as they were.
- **Printing.** Measured at a US Letter portrait width (740 pixels: 8.5 inches less Chrome's default margins), with `newLook` on, the seed's printed crosstable fits with its three rounds. Adding rounds one at a time, a crosstable whose result cells keep their screen shape stops fitting at eight rounds (799 pixels wide at eight); with the print shape it still fits at 12, as today's crosstable does. No rule outside `data-look="new"` is added and `src/index.css` is unchanged: the print shape is utility classes on the cell and the table.
- **Judgement calls.** K reviews these calls, with those of steps 23 to 26, before `newLook` is turned on (see Follow-ups):
  - Two columns are pinned, the number or the place and the player's name, the second at the first's measured width, as the Live-C board pins Place and Player. Pinning the first column alone would keep the number on screen while the names scroll away; moving the number into the name's cell would change the columns the print and seed specs read.
  - The tables under the new look are drawn through the primitives, so the row header and the pinned columns come from them and the tables are their first real callers; the branch for today is the old function, kept whole.
  - A row's tint goes through `--row-tint`, so a pinned cell lays the same tint over its opaque surface; first place's tint is written with the brand gold's theme colour, so a browser that cannot mix colours gets the fallback today's `bg-lca-gold/5` gets. The primitive's hover tint now reaches the chess rows under the new look, which helps a reader follow a row across a wide crosstable.
  - The result tints are the ok, bad and closed tints with tone shade words, not the status tokens (in the live look the full words are gold and every status tint is the panel) and not new win, loss and draw tokens (the live look's `--win`, `--loss` and `--draw` exist in no other look, and a token in every look would change `src/index.css` outside the new look). In the live look the tints are the panel, as the Looks-7 tile draws its crosstable cells, so there the words' colour, the letter and the mark carry the result.
  - A draw's words are the ink on the closed tint, as the board's dark slate on grey, so a win, a loss and a draw read at full strength and the games not played are the muted ones. In the light look the closed tint (`#f7f8fa`) is fainter than the board's grey; the letter carries the draw.
  - A forfeit, a bye and a round not played show no mark, since no colour was played (the server's tiebreaks count no colour for a forfeit either), as the board draws X20 and F7; a forfeit's words name the opponent and no colour.
  - The cell's words are hidden text and its parts are hidden from screen readers, rather than an `aria-label` on the span, which ARIA 1.2 does not allow; axe accepts it, and every screen reader reads hidden text.
  - A missing tiebreak's hyphen has "none yet" as hidden text rather than an `aria-label`: a span may not carry one, and not every screen reader reads one on a cell.
  - The place stays in the sans face, as the board's Place is; the pairing number, the rating, the points, W-D-L, the tiebreaks and the result cells take the monospace face.
  - The figures keep today's centring and sizes (points at `text-sm`, tiebreaks and totals at `text-xs`) rather than the Live-C board's right-aligned 13px figures with the points at 15px. The monospace face and tabular numerals make figures of the same length line up, but centred figures of different lengths (3 over 2½ in the seeded standings) do not line up by place. Right-aligning them under `newLook` is a small change for K to decide.
  - A selected row under the pointer: the hover rule outranks the selected rule for `--row-tint`, so the pinned cells of a selected row take the half-strength hover tint while the row keeps the full selected tint. No chess table selects a row, so nothing shows it today; a pinned cell also paints `bg-surface`, so a box that sits on a ground other than the surface needs `bg-surface` in `containerClassName` (all three callers pass it). Both are noted for the first page that selects rows or places a pinned table on another ground.
  - The key is a list naming each letter and the two marks in words at 12px, rather than today's 11px line with middle dots, below the 12px the boards use.
  - On paper a result cell gives up its 58px box and may wrap, and the pinned cells and a bounded header are static, so a wide crosstable stays on the page rather than running off it.
  - Each table's box under the new look is a named region in the tab order, as `ScrollPanel` is, even where the table fits, so the keyboard can scroll it on a phone and axe's `scrollable-region-focusable` passes; a table that fits is one more stop for Tab.
  - No page uses `bounded` yet: each page adopts it when its workstream rebuilds it, as with `ScrollPanel`, so the fixture page draws it.
  - `ResultsEntry` gets `scope="col"` under `newLook` only, where the two tables get it with `newLook` off too, since the attribute without the switch is allowed for those two alone.
  - In the browser test the live look is drawn on `main` as a live scope is drawn, with `data-theme="live"` and its own ground and words: `main` paints no ground of its own, and the live words on the white page would test nothing.

### Step 28: the rook mark and the official logo

The ninth step of checkpoint D. It builds the two brand components of decision D1 and puts them where their places exist today. `LcaMark` is the rook-shaped Louisiana mark, the favicon's one path in the colour of the words around it; `LcaLogo` is the board-voted full-colour logo, the file the header and the footer have always drawn, drawn as it is. Under `newLook` the current header shows the rook mark at every width, the footer keeps the official logo through `LcaLogo`, and `/about` shows the official logo in a frame near its heading. With `newLook` off `Navbar.tsx`, `Footer.tsx` and `AboutPage.tsx` draw the markup they drew, to the attribute: unlike step 27, nothing on those three is drawn differently without the switch, so `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unchanged. The LCA status tag's rook is the one change that does not depend on the switch: StatusBadge's status path is drawn by no page (the fixture page, which exists only in the accessibility test build, is its one caller) and has never read the switch. Nothing under `public/`, `functions/`, `workers/` or `index.html` changes, and `src/pages/AdminEmailPage.tsx` is left as it is (see Follow-ups). The base was `df62ca3` (step 27); nothing is committed yet.

- **Files.**
  - New: `src/components/LcaMark.tsx` and `src/components/LcaLogo.tsx`, each exporting its component and its props type only, `test/unit/lca-mark.test.tsx` (36), `test/unit/brand-placements.test.tsx` (12: the header, the footer and `/about` with `newLook` off and on in one file), its fixture `test/unit/fixtures/brand-today.ts` (today's markup of the three, rendered from the files at `df62ca3` before they changed), `test/unit/redesign-status-step28.test.ts` (25: these records) and `test/a11y/brand.spec.ts` (13 tests on each build; 10 run on `a11y-on` and 6 on `a11y-off`, each skipping on the build it does not check).
  - Changed: `src/components/layout/Navbar.tsx`, `src/components/layout/Footer.tsx`, `src/pages/AboutPage.tsx`, `src/components/StatusBadge.tsx` (the LCA tag's rook) and `docs/redesign/REDESIGN_SPEC.md` (the step's sentence in the WS01 deviations list).
  - Tests changed: `test/unit/status-badge.test.tsx` (51, 50 before: the two pins that said the LCA tag has no mark now count its rook among the eight marks, and a test of the rook is added) and `test/unit/browser-test-wiring.test.ts` (89, 87 before: the two new specs join its dash and wording check).
  - Tests added once the step was built: `test/unit/brand-edges.test.tsx` (32: every status tag drawn the same with `newLook` off and on, every status but the LCA tag drawing the tag it drew at `df62ca3` mark for mark and the LCA tag that tag with the rook before its words, however its words are given; the two components given props forced past their types; two named marks on one page; the header with its phone menu open, signed out and signed in; where in `src` the rook, the logo, its file and `public/lca-logo.jpg` are drawn or named; the step's files linted with the site's own rules; and the shape of this file round the step, the acceptance table's four cells in every row and the step's entries after step 27's) and `test/a11y/brand-edges.spec.ts` (10 tests on each build; 9 run on `a11y-on` and 6 on `a11y-off`, each skipping on the build it does not check: the header's mark at 320, 639, 640, 767, 768, 1023 and 1024 pixels, the header in forced colours with a light and a dark theme, the LCA tag's rook and the official logo in forced colours, the LCA tag's box in every look at four widths, the About frame in the base and heritage looks with a light and a dark device, axe at 390 pixels with a dark device, the header and the footer on twelve pages at 390 and 320 pixels, and the site's icon as served).
- **LcaMark** (`src/components/LcaMark.tsx`). The one-colour mark of D1, for the header at every width and tight spaces: the one path of `public/favicon.svg` (196 characters, from `M5 7.1H16.6V23H28.3` to `V7.1Z`), as it is there, in the favicon's 100 by 100 box. It has no colour of its own: the SVG is filled with `currentColor`, so it takes the colour it inherits from the element around it, the colour of its words (the header's `text-white`, the LCA tag's words colour); a caller colours that element and never the mark, since a colour set on an SVG itself is kept when forced colours replace the page's colours (see the header); the favicon's two fills, the navy `#1a2744` and `#ffd54a` under a dark device, which no site token has, are not copied. Its `title` is "Louisiana Chess Association" unless another is given: given words it is an image named by them, an SVG with `role="img"` and a `title` element, which React keeps inside the SVG rather than moving to the page's head; given an empty or blank title it is decoration, with no title and no role, hidden from screen readers, which is how the header and the LCA tag draw it, since words beside it name the place. Its `className` sets its size, 36 pixels (the kit's `.k-mark`) when none is given, and never its colour; its `slot` gives it `data-slot="status-mark"` where it is a status tag's mark, and it is `lca-mark` everywhere else. It takes no fill, colour or style, and its type refuses each. The path is unexported in the file, which exports the component and its props type only, for the only-export-components rule; step 31's Seal draws `LcaMark` inside its own SVG rather than copying the path.
- **LcaLogo** (`src/components/LcaLogo.tsx`). The board-voted full-colour logo of D1, for roomy places: one img of `src/assets/lca-logo.webp`, the file `Navbar.tsx` and `Footer.tsx` have always imported (400 by 399 pixels, 16,468 bytes), drawn as it is, with `data-slot="lca-logo"`. It takes `width`, `height` and `alt` ("Louisiana Chess Association" unless other words are given, an empty string where the name stands beside it) and nothing else: no class, style, colour, filter or opacity, and no other file, so a caller can neither recolour nor swap the logo, and the type refuses each. Its shape is its own, the footer's `rounded-xl` corners and `object-contain`, with nothing laid over it and no blend. The file is a lossy picture with no transparency, its corners and its top and bottom edges `#ffffff` and its sides no more than 12 levels from white in any channel, so it brings its own white ground wherever it is drawn, as D1's "set it on white or cream" asks. The site's base styles give every image `height: auto`, so the logo is drawn at the width given and at the height of the picture's own shape (55.86 pixels at 56 wide), its `height` holding the box while the file loads.
- **The header.** Under `newLook` the brand link's image is replaced by `LcaMark`, and nothing else changes: the link, its two-line name ("Louisiana Chess" over "Association") and the 64-pixel navy bar stay as they are, and nothing hides the name at any width, so the mark shows at every width, as D1 asks, with no width rule to build. The mark is white, 14.81:1 on `#1a2744`, where a graphic needs 3:1 (WCAG 1.4.11), 28 pixels on a phone and 30 pixels from 768 pixels up (`size-7 md:size-[30px]`, its one class), the sizes of the decided boards (Nav-Mobile-Final's app bar mark and Nav-Header-Final's `.nf-rook`). Its white is the header's own `text-white`, which it inherits, with no colour class on the mark or on the link between them: a browser keeps a colour set on an SVG itself when forced colours (a Windows contrast theme) replace the page's colours, and the header's navy is replaced with the theme's ground, so a mark given a `text-white` of its own stayed white on the light theme's white, 1.00:1, while the inherited white is replaced with the brand words' forced link colour, `rgb(0, 0, 159)` on white (13.99:1) and `rgb(255, 255, 0)` on black (19.56:1). Without forced colours the header is navy in every look and in dark mode, so the mark is white in all of them. It is decoration, hidden from screen readers, so the link is named once, by its words, "Louisiana Chess Association"; today it is named twice, by the image's words and by the name. With `newLook` off the header draws today's image, the same element in the same place. The link's `aria-label`, the squeeze and the one-row rule of the brief's header are WS02's (see Follow-ups).
- **The footer.** Under `newLook` the brand block draws `LcaLogo` at 56 pixels, today's size and corners, beside the name and straight on the navy, with no plate: the file's own white square is the white ground D1 asks for, as today's image's is. Its words are empty (`alt=""`), since the name beside it says them, so a screen reader hears the name once; the choice is made in one place, the footer's one call, with a comment. The decided footer board (Nav-Footer-Final, note 1 and Q9) draws the logo on a 44-pixel cream plate; the brief's footer line keeps the official logo with no plate named, and K settled Q9 against the plate for the header, so the footer has none either, a call for K's eye (see Judgement calls). Today's image took five lines and the branch for the new look takes four, the import of `LcaLogo` taking the fifth, so the column heading the gold guard pins stays on line 94.

The frame is the surface with a hairline and padding, in tokens, and no `bg-white`: the surface is white in the base look, `#fffdf8` (cream) in the heritage look, and the dark surface in dark mode, where the file's own white square still carries the white. No keep entry was needed, so the white mats' pinned counts did not move. The navy Get involved box and the page's words are as they were. Step 31 keeps this placement inside the heritage layout.

### Step 29: the still hero picture and nothing that moves on by itself

The tenth step of checkpoint D. Under `newLook` the homepage hero no longer moves on by itself: it is one still picture of slide 1, under the darkening, tint, gradient and label its slide had, with today's words, buttons and figures, and `HeroSlideshow`, whose timer moved the slides on every 5 seconds, is not drawn, so the page sets no timer. The picture comes from the image pipeline the step builds: `scripts/images.mjs`, run by hand with sharp, writes each slide photo as AVIF and WebP at 640, 1024 and 1600 pixels into `src/assets/generated`, 30 committed files of 200,000 bytes or less, so a build never needs sharp, and `ResponsiveImage` draws one as a `<picture>` from which the browser takes the smallest file the screen needs. A test reads every timer in `src`, and `npm run perf:home` measures `/` in the lab. With `newLook` off `HomePage.tsx` draws today's slideshow to the attribute, so `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unchanged. Nothing under `public/`, `functions/`, `workers/` or `index.html` changes, `src/assets/hero.png` is left as it is and out of the pipeline, and nothing is added to `test/a11y/known-violations.json`. The base was `c534742` (step 28); nothing is committed yet.

- **Files.**
  - New: `scripts/images.mjs` (the image script, run by hand), the 30 files in `src/assets/generated`, `src/components/ResponsiveImage.tsx` (its component and its props type only), `scripts/perf-home.ts` (the lab check), `test/unit/image-budget.test.ts` (38), `test/unit/no-rotation.test.ts` (19), `test/unit/hero-no-autoplay.test.tsx` (8: the hero with `newLook` on and off in one file), its fixture `test/unit/fixtures/home-hero-today.ts` (today's hero, rendered from `HomePage.tsx` at `c534742` before it changed), `test/unit/hero-images-edges.test.tsx` (56: `ResponsiveImage` on its own, the hero's `sizes`, the hero while the lists below it fail, the timers when the page goes, what a build carries of the generated files, what those files hold, the lab check kept on this computer and to the servers it starts, and `test/a11y/baseline.json` kept as it was recorded, its sample of `/` the hero's first Button), `test/unit/redesign-status-step29.test.ts` (27: these records), `test/a11y/home.spec.ts` (12 tests on each build; 7 run on `a11y-on` and 5 on `a11y-off`, each skipping on the build it does not check) and `test/a11y/home-edges.spec.ts` (4 tests on each build; 4 run on `a11y-on` and 1 on `a11y-off`: the file each window and density takes, the picture in its box, the hero while the lists fail, and the 30 files decoded beside their photos).
  - Changed: `src/pages/HomePage.tsx`, `src/vite-env.d.ts` (a module declaration for `.avif` files, without which the compiler refuses their import), `package.json` (sharp 0.34.5 and lighthouse 13.5.0 as exact development packages, and the `images` and `perf:home` scripts), `package-lock.json` and `docs/redesign/REDESIGN_SPEC.md` (the step's sentence in the WS01 deviations list).
  - Tests changed: `test/unit/goldText.test.ts` (its two pins of the trophy beside the Tournaments heading move from `HomePage.tsx` line 208 to 259, below the lines the step adds), `test/unit/browser-test-wiring.test.ts` (91, 89 before: the two new specs join its dash and wording check) and `test/unit/brand-edges.test.tsx` (its check that step 28's deviation comes right after step 27's now lets a later step's follow it).
- **ResponsiveImage** (`src/components/ResponsiveImage.tsx`). A picture in the sizes and formats the image script writes: a `<picture>` with an AVIF source and a WebP source, each listing its files by width (`srcset`) with the `sizes` the caller gives, so the browser takes, in the first format it can show, the smallest file that covers the space the picture fills at the screen's density. The img inside carries the source photo's own width and height, so the page holds the picture's shape before a file arrives and nothing moves when one does. The page's largest picture, the one a visitor sees first, is marked `priority`: it is fetched ahead of other pictures (`fetchpriority="high"`) and loaded at once (`loading="eager"`); every other picture waits until it nears the screen (`loading="lazy"`). The caller imports the files it draws one by one, so a build carries only the files its pages use, and must also give the img's own `src` (`fallback`): the photo in a format every browser shows, taken only by a browser that can show neither AVIF nor WebP (an older Safari, for one) and never fetched by one that takes a source. The hero gives slide 1's own JPEG, the file the slideshow draws. It takes the picture's words (`alt`, empty for decoration), a class and a style, which the hero uses for its darkening. The file exports the component and its props type only, for the only-export-components rule; its type for one file is not exported.
- **The image script** (`scripts/images.mjs`, `npm run images`). Run by hand with sharp 0.34.5, never by a build or a test: it writes each of `LCA_Slide_1.jpg` to `LCA_Slide_5.jpg` (2048 pixels wide, 2,171,020 bytes in all) as AVIF and as WebP at 640, 1024 and 1600 pixels wide into `src/assets/generated`, named `LCA_Slide_<n>-<width>.avif` and `.webp`, each at its photo's own shape. `src/assets/hero.png` is left out, because no page or file uses it. Each file must be 200,000 bytes or less, which holds either reading of the brief's 200 KB; the script checks every file it writes and stops with an error, once it has written them all, when one is over. Its quality table: AVIF at 60 and WebP at 75 for every file but four, slide 3 at 1600 pixels as AVIF at 55 (195,594 bytes) and WebP at 48 (192,900 bytes), and slide 5 at 1600 pixels as AVIF at 57 (188,139 bytes) and WebP at 60 (191,364 bytes); at the usual qualities those four are 228,891, 264,114, 210,463 and 229,892 bytes. The largest of the others is slide 1's 1600 pixel WebP, 175,804 bytes, and the file a 390 pixel phone at three times density takes for the hero, slide 1's 1600 pixel AVIF, is 162,969. Both formats are written at effort 4, the AVIF with its colour at half resolution (4:2:0), as the photos' own JPEG files and every lossy WebP store it, where sharp's default for AVIF is full resolution; at the same quality that makes each AVIF file 6 to 11 per cent smaller. Nothing is copied from the photos but their pixels: no camera data and no colour profile. Slides 2, 3 and 5 carry a Display P3 profile, which sharp uses to turn their colours into sRGB, the colour space of every file written. Two runs wrote the same 30 files byte for byte, in 51 seconds each.
- **The hero under newLook.** `HomePage.tsx` draws `HomeHero` on its own when `newLook` is on and `HeroSlideshow` when it is off. `HomeHero` holds the hero once: the outer element marked `data-ground="dark"`, still the one marked element in the file and on `/`, the words, buttons and figures over the photo, and, only when the slideshow draws it, the track of five slides and their controls. Under the new look `HeroSlideshow` is not drawn, so the page sets no timer (no `setInterval` and no `setTimeout`), and the hero has no slide control: no arrows, no dots and no track that moves. In the photo's place `HomeHero` draws slide 1 through `ResponsiveImage`, marked `priority`, at the photo's own shape (2048 by 1237), with slide 1's own JPEG, the file the slideshow draws, as the img's fallback, and a `sizes` of `(max-width: 695px) 696px, 100vw`: the picture covers the 420 pixel high hero (`absolute inset-0 h-full w-full object-cover`, centred as `bg-cover bg-center` was), so on a screen narrower than 696 pixels it is drawn 696 pixels wide and on a wider one across the screen, which is what that `sizes` says. Over it lie the slide's own layers, unchanged: the photo's darkening (grayscale 35 per cent, brightness 0.55 and contrast 1.05, with a scale of 1.04, now one style the slideshow's photos share), the navy tint `bg-brand-navy/55 mix-blend-multiply`, the gradient to `to-black/10` at the top, and slide 1's label, "Louisiana Chess Community", in its pill at the top right. The picture is decoration (`alt=""`), as the slideshow's backgrounds were. What the tests read of the file holds: `contrast.test.ts` and `semantic-map-grounds.test.ts` find one marked element in it with a `bg-brand-navy/55` layer inside and the darkening as written, and one light gold pill on the photo; the seven `HomePage.tsx` entries of the raw colour allowlist keep their nine uses, each class written once; `FEATURES` is imported as `Footer.tsx` imports it; `StatusDot` and the page's one `fetch` stay. The new parts add 51 lines above the trophy beside the Tournaments heading, which `goldText.test.ts` pinned at line 208, so its two pins move to line 259.
- **The hero with the switch off.** With `newLook` off `HeroSlideshow` keeps its state and its two timers, as today, and draws `HomeHero` with its slides, which is today's hero to the attribute: `test/unit/hero-no-autoplay.test.tsx` holds it to the literals in `test/unit/fixtures/home-hero-today.ts`, rendered from `HomePage.tsx` at `c534742` before the step changed it, as the page opens and 5 seconds later on the second slide, and to the first again after 25 seconds; a dot still shows its slide and starts the 5 seconds again. The reduced-motion rule is the new look's alone (`:root[data-look="new"]`), so with the switch off a visitor who asks for reduced motion still gets the slideshow, as today, and AC4 is proved on `a11y-on`. `test/a11y/baseline.ts` reads `/` with `newLook` off, where the hero's first Button, "Find a tournament", is today's in the same place, so `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unchanged.
- **The timer test.** `test/unit/no-rotation.test.ts` reads every `.ts` and `.tsx` file under `src` (not TSX alone: the price clock is a hook in a `.ts` file) with the TypeScript parser, so it counts calls, not a type such as `ReturnType<typeof setInterval>` or a word in a comment. Every `setInterval`, bare or called on `window`, `globalThis` or `self`, must be named in its allowlist by its file, with its reason and its number of calls, which must match, so a new one fails and a removed one must take its entry down. There are four entries and five calls: HomePage, two (the slideshow with `newLook` off, both inside `HeroSlideshow`, which the test checks is drawn only then, listed until WS03 deletes it), AdminEmailPage, one (the list of campaigns read again every 3 seconds while one is sending), AnnualMeetingPage, one (the countdown's clock, once a minute) and useNow, one (the price clock in `src/hooks/useNow.ts`, every 30 seconds). A `setTimeout` or `requestAnimationFrame` that runs again by itself fails wherever it is: one whose callback is, or calls, the function it sits in, and one in an effect that runs again once its callback sets a state. An effect runs again on a state its dependency list names, or on one a value in the list is read from (`const slide = slides[index]`, through `useMemo` or not, followed through the component's own values as far as they go); an effect with no dependency list, with a list that is not written out, or with a list naming a function, object or list written in the component without a hook to keep it (made anew on every render) runs after every render, so any state its timer sets makes a loop. The callback is followed through the functions the file defines, so a helper between them changes nothing, and a function given to `useCallback`, or written as a class method or field, goes by its name. What the test does not read is a timer that runs again only by way of another file, a prop or a context, or through a class component's lifecycle methods; those are for review. The seven one-shot timers in the tree (a debounce, a reload, a give-up, a scroll, a copied message, a map listener's removal and a released download address) pass. It fails on each case planted in a temporary tree, the timers' names put together when the test runs: a `setInterval` in a TSX page, one in a `.ts` hook called on `window`, a timer that schedules itself, one whose callback calls the function it sits in, a frame that asks for the next, a slideshow on an effect that lists its state, a carousel whose effect runs a helper on the timer or hands it a reducer's dispatch, a timer that a `useCallback`, a class method or a class field schedules again, two functions that call each other through the timer, an effect with no dependency list and one with a list that is not written out, an effect that lists a value read from the state (directly and through `useMemo`), an effect that lists a function made anew on every render (an arrow function and a function declaration), a third call in `HomePage.tsx`, and an entry left up after its calls are gone. It passes the one-shot cases planted beside them: a debounce whose helper sets only what its effect does not depend on, a message a helper clears once, an effect with an empty list, one whose list names only a prop, one that names a function kept by `useCallback`, and one that names a figure read from an object made in the component.
- **The lab check** (`scripts/perf-home.ts`, `npm run perf:home`). Run with `node --no-warnings --experimental-strip-types`, like `scripts/check-bundle.ts`; ESLint reads it with the TypeScript rules, and no compiler project includes it. It builds `dist-a11y-on` and the seeded local database as `npm run test:a11y` does, through `buildVariant` and `buildDatabase` from `test/a11y/global-setup.ts`, whose import runs nothing (local only, never a remote database), serves the build with `wrangler pages dev` on 127.0.0.1:8801 with the same fake settings and empty secrets (`a11yServerArgs`), starts Chromium headless with a debugging port, runs Lighthouse 13.5.0 on `/` through it, three times unless told, and stops both. Before it builds anything it checks that nothing listens on a port the test servers use (8801 and 8802, and their inspector ports 9231 and 9232), and stops if something does, so it measures only a server it started, as `playwright.config.ts` does with `reuseExistingServer: false`, and does not make the build and the database again under a server that is serving them; it checks the server's two ports once more just before starting it, and counts an answer from `/` only while that server runs. The server and the browser run in process groups of their own, so it stops them itself: when it ends, when the browser has not opened its debugging port after 30 seconds, and when it is stopped by Ctrl-C, by SIGTERM (which `timeout` sends and npm passes on) or by SIGHUP, asking each to stop and killing what still runs 10 seconds later. A server answering on 8801 and a listener on 9232, each planted before a run, stopped it within a second, before any build; SIGTERM, SIGHUP and SIGINT sent in the middle of a run, and a stand-in browser that never opened its port, each left no process, port or profile folder behind. For each run it prints the performance score, the five metrics, the LCP element with its time and how that breaks down, the pictures and the largest files fetched, and the insights that found something with what they name; then the scores and their median. The score is a measurement written down here, not a gate, and the script never fails on one. The browser is the one the browser tests use, `PW_CHROMIUM_PATH` when it is set and otherwise Playwright's own Chromium, started by the script with the settings Lighthouse's own launcher gives it (and without its sandbox when run by root, as in a container) rather than through chrome-launcher, so the check needs no package beyond lighthouse; and it refuses every host but 127.0.0.1, as the accessibility tests do, so neither the browser's own calls home nor anything outside enters the measurement. Its header says never to run it beside `npm run test:a11y` or beside itself, since they share the port, the build folder and the database folder. `--reuse` serves the build and the database as they are, `--runs` sets the number of runs and `--out` keeps each run's full report.
- **The places left for later.** WS03 replaces the hero and deletes `HeroSlideshow` with what only the slideshow needs: its timers and their allowlist entry, its controls' four raw colour allowlist entries, the dots' entries in `test/a11y/known-violations.json`, the flag-off half of `test/unit/hero-no-autoplay.test.tsx` and its fixture, and the photos of slides 2 to 5, which only the slideshow draws; slide 1's JPEG stays while a picture of slide 1 needs its fallback (see Follow-ups, "WS03 deletes HeroSlideshow (step 29)"). The other 24 generated files wait for a page that draws them, and none ships until one is imported; WS03's one photo is slide 1 at 200 KB on a 390 pixel phone (`docs/redesign/DESIGN_REPLAN_phase0.md` line 376), which these files already meet. What the lab check found beyond the hero is WS03's, and the lab run on a preview that shows the still hero is K's (see Follow-ups).

### Step 30: the heritage look's type, loaded only inside its scope

The eleventh step of checkpoint D. It adds the Heritage Club look's type: `src/styles/themes/heritage.css`, which `ThemeScope` imports lazily the first time a heritage scope renders, holds the latin files of Libre Caslon Display and Source Serif 4, a Georgia fallback face for each, the `--font-display` and `--font-text` tokens, the type rules a heritage page opts into on its own elements (display headings and figures, a document's reading column, the 2px radius of cards, tables and mats, and small capitals), and the base look's accent face switched off. Every rule that sets a face, a size, a radius or a measure waits for `newLook`, as the heritage colours do. Nothing draws a heritage scope but the fixture page, which exists only in the browser-test build and carries none of the hooks, so the site draws what it drew before: the production build is the same, byte for byte, and `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unchanged. Nothing under `public/`, `functions/`, `workers/`, `src/pages`, `src/components/ui` or `index.html` changes, and `src/index.css`, on the step's list, is not changed. The base was `abe7d07`, the commit after step 29 that gave each browser-test server a database of its own; nothing is committed yet.

- **Files.**
  - Packages: `@fontsource/libre-caslon-display` 5.3.0 and `@fontsource-variable/source-serif-4` 5.3.0, both published Sunday, July 19, 2026, exact-pinned under `dependencies` with `npm install --save-exact --before=2026-10-07`; `package-lock.json` gains the two packages and their two root lines and nothing else. `@fontsource-variable/source-serif-4` 5.3.1, published Sunday, October 11, 2026, is under two weeks old and is not used.
  - New: `src/styles/themes/heritage.css`, `test/unit/heritage-css.test.ts` (21), `test/unit/heritage-budget.test.ts` (11), `test/unit/heritage-glyphs.test.ts` (14), the helpers `test/unit/helpers/woff2.ts` (the WOFF2 table and character map reader of `test/unit/geist-mono-glyphs.test.ts`, copied and given every table tag, with readers of a font's features and metrics) and `test/unit/helpers/heritageFaces.ts` (the faces a stylesheet declares, followed into its imports and to their files), and `test/unit/redesign-status-step30.test.ts` (31: these records).
  - Changed: `src/components/ThemeScope.tsx`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json` and `docs/redesign/REDESIGN_SPEC.md` (the step's sentence in the WS01 deviations list).
  - Tests changed: `test/unit/theme-scope-wiring.test.ts` (21, 19 before), `test/unit/theme-scope.test.tsx` (33, 31 before), `test/unit/theme-scope-edges.test.tsx` (15, 13 before) and `test/unit/redesign-status-step24.test.ts` (its date line pattern lets a step in the 30s come first, as the patterns of steps 25 to 29 already did).
  - Edge tests: `test/unit/heritage-edges.test.ts` (28: the stylesheet built with the site's own Vite config as one `heritage-<hash>.css` beside exactly the four files its faces name, each the package's own bytes, every `url()` an emitted file, the fallback overrides, the registration of the display size, the guard and every rule's meaning kept, in no layer, and the roman files within the budget, each check failing on a planted build; ThemeScope's import of it built as a file loaded on demand, sharing no file with the live one; the whole site built as it is deployed, with no heritage file or face, and as the browser tests build it, with the stylesheet named only by the fixture page's chunk and a loader that adds a stylesheet's `<link>` once, so that after a failed load a second import fetches nothing; the two packages pinned at 5.3.0 with the lock file and the installed copies agreeing; each file's typographic metrics in use, equal to its hhea metrics, and the header's arithmetic; the tabular figures the reader asks for; and no variant or important form of the accent class under `src`) and `test/unit/heritage-scope-edges.test.tsx` (17: every rule matched by the DOM's own selector engine against a page of hooks and controls, with `newLook` on and off, in dark mode, in the other looks and outside any scope, and against the fixture page itself, which takes nothing but the scope's tokens; the accent class taking another face everywhere inside heritage, a base scope included; each check failing on a planted stylesheet; and a heritage scope taken away before its file arrives).
- **The faces.** `heritage.css` imports `@fontsource/libre-caslon-display/latin-400.css`, the package's latin face alone (400, its one weight, with no range), and declares Source Serif 4's two latin faces itself, since the variable package has no stylesheet for one subset: the roman and the italic of the weight axis (200 to 900), each naming its file by its package path (`@fontsource-variable/source-serif-4/files/source-serif-4-latin-wght-normal.woff2`, which the package's exports allow), with the package's own descriptors and latin range. The bundler resolves both paths and emits the files. The files that add the optical size axis (122,360 bytes for the latin roman) are not used, nor are the other subsets (latin-ext, cyrillic, greek and vietnamese), so a letter outside the latin files is drawn from the fallback. The italic is declared and fetched only when italic text is set in the face; Caslon has no italic.
- **The fallback faces.** Three `@font-face` rules of the device's Georgia (`local()`), named `Libre Caslon Display Fallback` and `Source Serif 4 Fallback` (roman and italic, the italic from Georgia Italic), each with `size-adjust`, `ascent-override`, `descent-override` and `line-gap-override`. Georgia's metrics are from `@capsizecss/metrics` 4.3.0 (published Tue, Sep 15, 2026), read as data and not installed: 2048 units per em, ascent 1878, descent 449, line gap 0 and an average width of 913, 931 in Georgia Italic. The faces' own are read from the 5.3.0 latin files: 1000 units per em; Libre Caslon Display ascent 970, descent 266, line gap 0; Source Serif 4 ascent 1036, descent 335, line gap 0 in both styles. The average width is Capsize's, each character's advance weighted by how often it occurs in English text: recomputed from the 5.3.0 files with the weights of `@capsizecss/unpack` 4.0.1, it is 374 for Caslon, 479 for Source Serif 4 and 441 for its italic, the numbers Capsize publishes. So size-adjust is (face width / 1000) / (Georgia width / 2048), and each override the face's metric over its 1000 units, divided by size-adjust: Caslon 83.89%, ascent 115.62%, descent 31.71% and line gap 0%; Source Serif 107.45%, 96.42%, 31.18% and 0%; its italic 97.01%, 106.79%, 34.53% and 0%. The arithmetic is in the file's header, and `test/unit/heritage-css.test.ts` holds each vertical override, times size-adjust, to the face's own file. The emitted `heritage-*.css` keeps all four descriptors of each face. The numbers are worked out, not measured: Georgia is not installed on the computer that built the step, and the layout shift of the swap is measured in step 31 (AC11; see Follow-ups). Android phones, ChromeOS and most Linux systems have no Georgia either, so there both fallback faces are skipped and the swap runs from the device's own serif, at its own size and metrics; step 31 measures that swap as well (see Follow-ups, "Georgia on the test computers (step 30)"). The stacks are Caslon, its fallback, Georgia and serif, and Source Serif, its fallback, Georgia and serif.
- **The tokens.** On the scope, under `newLook`: `--font-display` (Caslon's stack), `--font-text` (Source Serif's), `--font-accent`, pointed at `--font-text`, and `--display-size`, 2.25rem, the size a display heading takes when a page sets none. No other declaration names Caslon, and nothing but the display rule reads `--font-display`. `src/index.css` gains no token: its heritage blocks hold colours only, as before.
- **The hooks.** The elements the brief's rules are for have no handle yet, so the stylesheet names a small set that pages put on their own elements, `data-heritage` with one or more words: `display` on an h1 or an h2 (Caslon at 400, at the size `--display-size` gives, 2.25rem from the scope when a page sets none, and never under 2rem, 32 pixels: the property is registered with `@property` as a length or a percentage, so a value that is neither, such as the bare number React writes for a number in a style, counts as not set and the heading takes the size its scope or a wrapper gives, where unregistered it made the size invalid and the heading took the size of the text around it; a page sets it in rem or px or as a percentage, never in a unit of the heading's own font, which set on the heading makes its size depend on itself); `figure` on a display figure such as a founding year or a member count (the same, but never inside a table or on figures in the fixed-width face, so ratings, scores and tables keep theirs); `reader` on a document's reading column (Source Serif at 1.125rem, 18 pixels, with a line height of 1.6, lining and tabular figures and a measure of 37em); and `card`, `table` or `mat` for the 2px radius, which the About page's logo frame (`data-slot="lca-logo-mat"`) takes by its own slot. Nothing is styled by its tag alone, so an h1 or h2 without the hook, and every other element of a heritage page, keeps the base type. No rule reaches a control: buttons, fields, chips, status tags and dots, date blocks, result cells, the fixed-width face, the focus ring and the radius token keep the base look, and the scope's own element gets no face, so a form drawn in a base scope inside heritage keeps the site's own face. The reader passes its face to everything in it, so it goes on the text, not around a control; the file's header says so.
- **Small capitals.** `.small-caps`, inside a heritage scope under `newLook`, sets capitals at 0.85em with 0.07em of tracking, in the text face: neither latin file has small capitals (no smcp or c2sc feature, read from the files), and a browser asked for them with `font-variant-caps` would make them by shrinking capitals, so nothing in the file asks. The class takes its size from the text around it, and is for labels of 15px or more, never buttons or sentences, as the plan says.
- **The accent face.** Instrument Serif is the base look's `font-accent`, whose utility writes the family out rather than reading `--font-accent`, so pointing the token elsewhere would leave the class on it. Inside a heritage scope under `newLook` the class takes the text face, and on the scope's own element it takes the face of the page around it, so a scope given the class still keeps a form inside it in the site's face; the token points at `--font-text`. No file under `src` but `src/index.css` names the face, whose import stays there (`test/unit/tokens.test.ts` pins it), so it is declared on every page, as before, and fetched only where text is set in it.
- **The guard and the layer.** Every rule but the `@font-face` declarations and the registration of `--display-size` is written under `:root[data-look="new"] [data-theme="heritage"]`, the selector the heritage colours use, so with `newLook` off a heritage scope looks as it did, though the file loads on either build: `ThemeScope` names no switch (`test/unit/theme-scope-wiring.test.ts`). A registration cannot sit inside a rule, and it sets nothing on an element: it gives the property a type, and only the display rule, which waits for `newLook`, reads it. The rules are in no cascade layer, so they outrank the stylesheet's utility layer on the same element whatever its specificity: a hook keeps its face, its weight and its floor whatever classes the element also has, and the accent override wins over the utility.
- **ThemeScope.** `SCOPE_FONTS` gains a lazy import of `@/styles/themes/heritage.css` beside the live file, so a heritage scope imports the file on its first render, once for all heritage scopes. An import that fails is forgotten, as for live, so the next heritage scope calls `import()` again, but in a built site that call fetches nothing: Vite's preload helper remembers each stylesheet it has added and leaves the failed `<link>` in the page, so the second call resolves at once and the file stays missing until the page is reloaded (see Verify items, "The retry after a failed load (step 30)"). The comment beside the call says so. Its header comment says the file loads whatever the switches say and its rules wait for the new look, and that the file is asked for in an effect, after the scope's first paint, so an element that takes one of its type rules is drawn first in the base type and moves when the file arrives (see Deviations and Follow-ups). No other file names `heritage.css`.
- **The budget.** `test/unit/heritage-budget.test.ts` follows `heritage.css` the way the build does, into the package stylesheet it imports and to the files its faces name, and reads the real files: each family has one roman face, and its file is the package's latin file (every face of the package that names it covers the characters of English text, and the face the stylesheet writes keeps the package's range for it); the roman woff2 files come to 75,060 bytes, Caslon's 24,236 and Source Serif's 50,824, under 110,000, so either reading of 110 KB holds; the italic, 51,516 bytes, is named only by an italic face and is outside the sum; and Caslon's `.woff`, 20,048 bytes, comes after its woff2 in the face, so a browser that reads woff2 never fetches it, and is not counted. With italic text on a page the three files come to 126,576 bytes (see the Judgement calls). It fails on a planted extra file (the optical size file beside the weight one), a planted subset (Caslon's stylesheet of both subsets in place of the latin one), another subset in place of the latin file, the italic declared as roman and a range the package does not give.
- **The rules test.** `test/unit/heritage-css.test.ts` reads `heritage.css` as a browser applies it: the accent utility and token that `src/index.css` gives Instrument Serif, found by compiling its `@theme` blocks with Tailwind, are each overridden in the scope, on its descendants and on its own element, by an unlayered rule naming another face, and no file under `src` but `src/index.css` names Instrument Serif, nor any but `heritage.css` Caslon; Caslon is named only by `--font-display`, which only the display rule reads, for an h1 or h2 with the display hook or a display figure kept out of tables and the fixed-width face, at 400 and never under 32px; the display size it reads is registered as a length or a percentage, passed down, from 36px, at the top level, and set to 2.25rem on the scope, nothing else is registered, and every value a file under `src` gives it, in a style object, a class, a declaration or a `setProperty` call, is a length in rem or px or a percentage; `.small-caps` is capitals at 0.85em with 0.06em to 0.08em of tracking, and no rule asks for small capitals; no rule reaches a control anywhere in its selector, sets an outline, a shadow or the radius token, styles an element by its tag alone or gives the scope's own element a face; every rule but the faces and the registration waits for `newLook`, in no layer; the reader is Source Serif at 18px with a line height of 1.6, lining and tabular figures and a measure of 70 of its figure zeros, read from the font file (0.529em each); the radius goes to the four hooks only, at 2px; and the fallback faces are Georgia, one for each real face, with the four overrides. Each check fails on a planted stylesheet, the names put together when the test runs.
- **The glyphs.** `test/unit/heritage-glyphs.test.ts` reads the latin files the look names, Caslon's and Source Serif's roman and italic: each draws ½, the en dash, the minus sign, the ten digits and the no-break space, none draws the white or the black circle in its character map or its range, and in each package's full stylesheet the one face whose range covers ½ is the latin file the look names; neither face has smcp or c2sc. It closes the step 27 follow-up on the heritage faces' glyphs.
