- **Forced colours (step 28).** Found: Chromium gives every `svg` element `forced-color-adjust: preserve-parent-color`, so with forced colours on (a Windows contrast theme) an SVG keeps a colour set on itself and takes its parent's forced colour only when it has none of its own. With `text-white` on the header's mark, the light theme drew it `rgb(255, 255, 255)` on the header's forced `rgb(255, 255, 255)`, 1.00:1, and the dark theme kept it white rather than the forced link colour; with no colour on the mark it takes the link's forced colour, the brand words' colour, `rgb(0, 0, 159)` on white (13.99:1) and `rgb(255, 255, 0)` on black (19.56:1) (`test/a11y/brand-edges.spec.ts`). The LCA tag's rook never had a colour of its own and takes its words' forced colour on both builds.
- **Sideways scroll on a phone (step 28).** Measured in the browser on both builds, on fifteen public pages at 390 and 320 pixels: `/about` fits, and so do the header and the footer of every page; `/clubs` is 393 pixels wide at 390 and at 320, and `/scholastic` 334 at 320 (it fits at 390), the same with `newLook` off and on, so neither comes from this step; and `/about`, `/governance/board`, `/governance/bylaws` and `/governance/minutes` hold two `main` elements, `GovLayout`'s inside the site's, on both builds (see Follow-ups, "Pages wider than a phone, and a main inside a main").

- **Hero weight and auto-advance (step 29).** Confirmed, with more found. The five slides are 569,165, 303,072, 518,776, 290,367 and 489,640 bytes (2,171,020 in all), each 2048 pixels wide but of three shapes: 2048 by 1237 (slide 1), 2048 by 1536 (slides 2, 3 and 5) and 2048 by 882 (slide 4). `HeroSlideshow` called `setInterval(..., 5000)` at lines 53 and 56 (in `restart`, run by the arrows and dots, and in the effect, whose empty dependency list is a lint warning); nothing in `HomePage.tsx` or any file it draws paused it or read a reduced-motion setting, and the reduced-motion rule of `src/index.css` (lines 759 to 773) sets only CSS durations to zero, and only under `:root[data-look="new"]`, so it could not stop an interval even with the switch on. `src/assets/hero.png` is 343 by 361 pixels and 13,057 bytes, and no file under `src`, `test` or `scripts`, nor `index.html`, names it. The five photos were CSS backgrounds in a track that slides sideways, so all five were fetched on every visit, whichever slide showed (see "The hero's photos are backgrounds").
- **Other timers (step 29).** Confirmed, with a third found: `AdminEmailPage.tsx` line 196 (`setInterval(loadHistory, 3000)`, only while a campaign is sending) and `AnnualMeetingPage.tsx` line 80 (a 60 second clock for the countdown) move nothing on, and nor does `src/hooks/useNow.ts` line 17, a 30 second refresh of the price clock that `TournamentDetailPage.tsx` and `FamilyRegistrationPanel.tsx` draw, in a `.ts` file, which the plan's finding left out. The seven `setTimeout` calls run once: `UscfSearchInput.tsx` line 177 (a debounce), `FamilyRegistrationPanel.tsx` line 126 (a reload after 1200 ms), `ResetPasswordPage.tsx` line 91 (a give-up after 6 seconds), `TournamentsPage.tsx` line 456 (a scroll), `ScannerPage.tsx` lines 589 and 614, and `src/areas/clubs/clubMap.ts` line 84. A text search counts too many: `HomePage.tsx` line 47, `UscfSearchInput.tsx` line 160 and `ResetPasswordPage.tsx` line 51 name the timers in types, not calls, so the timer test reads calls with the TypeScript parser.
- **PageHero without any slideshow (step 29).** Confirmed: `src/components/PageHero.tsx` draws no image and no slideshow, its comment at line 19 reads "NOT for the HomePage slideshow hero", and 12 files import it; there is nothing to remove there.
- **The hero's photos are backgrounds (step 29).** Found: there was no img to turn into a `<picture>`. `HomePage.tsx` drew each photo as a div with `backgroundImage: url(...)` (line 73), darkened by an inline filter (line 74) and a scale of 1.04 (line 75), under the navy tint (line 79) and the gradient (line 81), in a box 420 pixels high (line 65), with the words and buttons a layer of their own over it (lines 88 to 112). `src` held 16 img elements in 13 files, none of them a hero, and no `<picture>`, `source` or `srcset`, so the still picture is the first on the site. It keeps the same darkening, as a style on the img, with the tint and the gradient over it, so the contrast test's reading of the photo, no lighter than `#40444c`, still holds, in a browser that shows neither AVIF nor WebP too, which takes the img's fallback, slide 1's JPEG.
- **The slides' shapes and sizes (step 29).** Measured: a 390 pixel phone at three times density needs 1170 pixels, and the picture covers a 420 pixel high box, so slide 1, 1.656 times as wide as it is high, is drawn 696 pixels wide on that phone, and the 1600 pixel file is taken with either `sizes`. At AVIF 60 and WebP 75, slides 3 and 5 at 1600 pixels are over 200,000 bytes and every other file is under; the step's quality table holds all 30 under it (see the step).
- **sharp is already installed (step 29).** Found: `node_modules/sharp` was 0.34.5, a development package of miniflare, wrangler's local runtime, which pins it exactly, with its Linux files in `node_modules/@img`; it writes AVIF (libheif 1.20.2 with aom 3.13.1) and WebP (libwebp 1.6.0). `package.json` did not list it. npm's latest is 0.35.5 (published Sun, Sep 27, 2026); 0.34.5 (Thu, Nov 6, 2025) is the copy the lockfile held, so `npm install --save-dev --save-exact sharp@0.34.5` added its line to `package.json` and to the lockfile's root entry and nothing else, with no second copy and no download.
- **The lab check's package (step 29).** Found: lighthouse was not installed. 13.5.0 (published Fri, Sep 18, 2026; it needs node 22.19 or later, where this computer runs 22.22.0 and CI's `node-version: '22'` takes the newest 22) was installed exact through the proxy with `--before` set to Wed, Oct 7, 2026; it brings 96 packages, chrome-launcher 1.2.2 among them, and shares axe-core 4.13.0 with `@axe-core/playwright`, so no package already in the lockfile changed, none runs an install script and no browser was downloaded. The `.ts` files in `scripts/` are in no compiler project, but ESLint reads them, so `scripts/perf-home.ts` lints clean.
- **The avif declaration (step 29).** Found: `src/vite-env.d.ts` declared svg, png, jpg, jpeg, webp and css files and no avif, and the site's compiler settings do not load the bundler's client types, so `tsc -b`, the first half of `npm run build`, refuses an import of a `.avif` file without a declaration; the step adds one. The bundler already emits AVIF and WebP files with hashed names (inlined only under 4,096 bytes, which none is), and no image plugin is configured.
- **What ships (step 29).** Measured: the six imports of slide 1's files, each on its own, put six more files in `dist/assets` (127 before, 133 after), where an `import.meta.glob` over `src/assets/generated` would put all 30 there (157), the build with `newLook` off included; `src/App.tsx` imports `HomePage.tsx` eagerly, so its six files are in every build.
- **The HomePage pins (step 29).** Found: seven tests read `HomePage.tsx`, and they set how it could change. `contrast.test.ts` expects one `data-ground="dark"` element there with no navy of its own, the filter string, `bg-brand-navy/55 mix-blend-multiply` and `to-black/10`, and one light gold pill on the photo; `goldText.test.ts` counts that pill once through its allowlist and pinned the trophy at line 208; `semantic-map-grounds.test.ts` expects one marked element in the file with a `bg-brand-navy/55` layer inside; `raw-colours.test.ts` counts seven classes and nine uses; `area-boundaries.test.ts` the one `fetch`; `base-components-scope.test.ts` the page's own `StatusDot`; and `brand-edges.test.tsx` that the page names neither brand component. So the marked element, the pill, the heading, the words, the two buttons and the three figures are written once, in `HomeHero`, and only the photo layers and the controls differ; the trophy's two pins moved to line 259, and no other pin or count moved. No test drew the home page before, so `hero-no-autoplay.test.tsx` draws it with the hoisted switch of `news-pinned-flag.test.tsx`, the sign-in context of `reminder-bell.test.tsx` and its two list requests answered.
- **The third interval (step 29).** Found: `src/hooks/useNow.ts` line 17 sets an interval in a `.ts` file, so the timer test reads `.ts` files as well as TSX, and its allowlist names it with the price clock's reason.
- **The img elements on / (step 29).** Measured in the browser: on `a11y-on`, `/` draws two img elements, the hero's picture (2048 by 1237) and the footer's `LcaLogo` (56 by 56), both with a width and a height; the header's brand mark is an SVG, and the Facebook band's compact variant draws no image. On `a11y-off` it draws two, the header's logo (`Navbar.tsx` line 240) and the footer's (`Footer.tsx` line 62), sized by their classes with no width or height attributes, today's markup, which the step leaves as it is (see Follow-ups). The one img `FacebookFeed.tsx` can draw (line 212, in the full variant on `/news`) has neither, and is not on `/`.
- **The known-violations list (step 29).** Found: `test/a11y/known-violations.json` lists 18 findings on `/`, nine a build: three colour-contrast lines and six target-size ones for the slideshow's dots (`.w-5` and Slide 1 to Slide 5). The step adds nothing and removes nothing: the slideshow stays with the switch off, so the `a11y-off` entries stay true, and `test/unit/browser-test-wiring.test.ts` fails unless the two builds list the same findings. With the switch on, `smoke.spec.ts` reports each `a11y-on` entry as listed but not found, a report and an annotation rather than a failure ("axe on / (a11y-on): 25 rules passed, 0 violation(s) found, 0 of them known."): the six for the dots, now that the still hero has no dots or arrows, and the three colour-contrast lines in the lists below the hero, which this step does not touch.
- **The picture before the script runs (step 29).** Found: `index.html` has no preload and is not this step's to change (a preload there would fire on every route, and with the switch off); the page is drawn by its script, whose main chunk is 509,728 bytes in the site's build, so the hero's img exists only once the script has run, `fetchpriority` and all. Lighthouse says so in every run ("Request is discoverable in initial document: no"), which goes to WS03 (see Follow-ups).
- **The baseline (step 29).** Confirmed: `test/a11y/baseline.ts` reads `/`, `/tournaments`, `/membership` and `/tournaments/fx-t-done` on `a11y-off`; on `/` the first Button in `main` is "Find a tournament" in the hero, today's with `newLook` off, so the comparison matches there, and `test/a11y/baseline.json` keeps its hash (`ce04ac06f0b3906739738413ef7aa3a21b0e8b0b9e3fe1c09dbca15f9f4dcbef`). `baseline.ts` and `baseline.json` are left for step 32; `home.spec.ts` waits for the hero's heading and picture, not for network idle alone, and asks nothing of `/tournaments/fx-t-done`. In the step's first two full runs the comparison failed with the lock signature, never on `/`: once on `/tournaments/fx-t-done`, drawn empty after a `SQLITE_BUSY` ("/tournaments/fx-t-done tableHeader: was found, now missing"), and once on `/tournaments` ("/tournaments button: was found, now missing"); and in the third, once these records were written, on `/tournaments/fx-t-done` again. Run on its own on the final code, after a fresh build and database, it printed "Baseline matches: no colour or font changed.", and `baseline.json` was never rewritten.
- **Records (step 29).** Found: the record tests of steps 18, 21, 23, 26, 27 and 28 look for those steps in the first 400 characters of this file; step 29's entry in the date line ("step 29: hero images; ", 22 characters) leaves step 18 ending at character 392, inside them, so no older entry was shortened. The blocks of steps 21 to 28 each run to the Decisions, so this one, after them, keeps their rules as well: no fixture route by its path, no placeholder in braces and none of the working words. `test/unit/brand-edges.test.tsx` held step 28's deviation to be the last; it now holds that step 28's comes right after step 27's and that any after it are later steps'. The Clearinghouse dates follow-up names `src/lib/clearinghouse.ts`, which step 17 moved to `src/areas/events/clearinghouse.ts`; a sentence there says so.
- **Lint, build and the browser runs (step 29).** Measured: lint 13 errors and 13 warnings before and after; the build sizes are under the step; the browser runs under Checks.
- **Counts (step 29).** The raw colour allowlist stays at 35 entries and 49 uses, the gold guard's places and the fixture page's 13 `[data-status]` elements and five Buttons are as they were, `test/a11y/known-violations.json` keeps its 18 entries, and `src` holds 17 img elements in 14 files, `ResponsiveImage`'s among them, and one `<picture>`.

- **Latin subset and smcp / c2sc (step 30).** Confirmed: no. Read from the 5.3.0 files themselves: Libre Caslon Display's latin 400 file has the GSUB features dnom, frac, liga and numr and the GPOS feature kern, with no smcp, c2sc, pnum, tnum or lnum; Source Serif 4's latin files with the weight axis, roman and italic, and its roman with the optical size axis as well, have GSUB ccmp, dnom, frac, liga, locl, numr, pnum and tnum and GPOS kern, mark and mkmk, with no smcp, c2sc, lnum or onum, so its figures are lining unless told otherwise and its tabular figures come through tnum. Caslon has no tabular figures, which fits the rule that keeps it off ratings, scores and tables. `.small-caps` uses the uppercase fallback the brief gives, and `test/unit/heritage-glyphs.test.ts` holds both faces to having no smcp or c2sc.
- **Heritage font budget (step 30).** Confirmed: Libre Caslon Display latin 400 is 24,236 bytes; Source Serif 4 latin with the weight axis, 50,824 (roman) and 51,516 (italic); its latin roman with the optical size axis as well, 122,360. Caslon and the roman weight file come to 75,060 bytes, which `test/unit/heritage-budget.test.ts` reads from the files the stylesheet names.
- **Font package names (step 30).** Confirmed: `@fontsource/libre-caslon-display` and `@fontsource-variable/source-serif-4` both resolve at 5.3.0, published Sunday, July 19, 2026 (94 days before the step), each with no dependencies; both are installed and exact-pinned.
- **The newest Source Serif (step 30).** Found: `@fontsource-variable/source-serif-4` 5.3.1 is its newest, published Sunday, October 11, 2026, ten days before the step and so under the two weeks a new package must be; 5.3.0 is pinned (see Follow-ups). 5.3.0 is `@fontsource/libre-caslon-display`'s newest.
- **What each package ships (step 30).** Found: Libre Caslon Display 5.3.0 has `latin-400.css` (the latin face alone, with no range), `latin-ext-400.css`, and `400.css` and `index.css` (both subsets, each with its range), and every face names a `.woff` after its `.woff2` (the latin one 20,048 bytes). Source Serif 4 Variable 5.3.0 calls its family `Source Serif 4 Variable`; `wght.css`, the same as `index.css`, declares six roman faces (cyrillic-ext, cyrillic, greek, vietnamese, latin-ext and latin, 181,072 bytes in all), `wght-italic.css` the same six in italic, and `opsz.css`, `opsz-italic.css`, `standard.css` and `standard-italic.css` the larger files. It has no stylesheet for one subset, but its exports allow `./files/*.woff2`, so a face can name the latin file by its package path, which the bundler resolves and emits. The latin range covers U+0000 to U+00FF and U+2000 to U+206F, so it holds ½, the en dash and the minus sign. Imported whole, the two packages' roman faces would come to 221,616 bytes, so the stylesheet names the latin files only, the files a browser fetches for English text, and the budget counts them (see the step's Judgement calls).
- **No font token for heritage (step 30).** Found: no `--font-display` existed in `src`, the tests or the scripts, the heritage blocks of `src/index.css` hold colours only, and no page uses the accent or heading font classes. The accent utility compiles to its family written out (`test/unit/token-utilities.test.ts`), and the built stylesheet holds both the token and the utility's own family, so the scope overrides the utility as well as the token. `--font-display` and `--font-text` are defined in `heritage.css` only.
- **The hooks (step 30).** Found: `Card` (`src/components/ui/card.tsx`) and `Table` (`src/components/ui/table.tsx`, whose box takes classes only) carry no `data-slot`, nor do `Input` and the other fields, where `Button`, the status tags and the chips do; no reader exists (the bylaws and minutes reader is WS14's), and the one mat is `data-slot="lca-logo-mat"` on `/about` (`src/pages/AboutPage.tsx` line 17). So the stylesheet names `data-heritage` words that pages put on their own elements, and the frame's slot.
- **The 32 px floor (step 30).** Found: CSS cannot test a rendered size. The pages draw h1 at 30 pixels below 640 pixels wide and 36 above (`src/components/PageHero.tsx` lines 95 and 99), the fixture page's h1 at 30 at every width and its h2s at 20, so a rule on every h1 from some width would put Caslon on headings under 32 pixels; the display hook carries its own size instead, never under 2rem. A value of `--display-size` that is not a length made that size invalid, so the heading took the size of the text around it; the property is registered with `@property` as a length or a percentage, so such a value counts as not set (see the step's Judgement calls).
- **Heritage waits for newLook (step 30).** Confirmed: the heritage colours wait for `:root[data-look="new"]`, and `ThemeScope` and `src/lib/themeScopes.ts` may name no switch (`test/unit/theme-scope-wiring.test.ts`), so `heritage.css` loads on either build and every rule in it but the `@font-face` declarations and the registration of `--display-size`, which sets nothing on an element, is written under `:root[data-look="new"] [data-theme="heritage"]`.
- **Georgia is not installed (step 30).** Found: this computer has no Georgia (`fc-list` lists DejaVu Serif, FreeSerif and Liberation Serif among its serif faces), so no browser here can draw the fallback faces, which report an error and give way to the next face; Georgia's metrics come from `@capsizecss/metrics` 4.3.0, read as data, and the overrides are worked out (see the step). Android phones, ChromeOS and most Linux systems have no Georgia either, so there the fallback faces are skipped as they are here (see Follow-ups, "Georgia on the test computers (step 30)").
- **No acceptance row (step 30).** Found: AC11, AC12 and AC13 are all step 31's, so the step has no row of its own. AC11 and AC12 each gain a sentence for it, AC11's in place of its clause "the heritage font chunk is step 30"; AC13 and AC8 are left as they were.
- **The glyph pin (step 30).** Confirmed: Libre Caslon Display's latin 400 file and Source Serif 4's latin files with the weight axis, roman and italic, each draw ½ (U+00BD), the en dash, the minus sign, the ten digits and the no-break space, and none draws the white or the black circle (U+25CB and U+25CF); in each package's full stylesheet the one face whose range covers ½ is the latin file the look names. `test/unit/heritage-glyphs.test.ts` holds it, and the follow-up on the heritage faces' glyphs is closed (see Follow-ups).
- **Production and the browser-test builds (step 30).** Confirmed: `src/pages/fixtures/ScopeFixturePage.tsx` is the only file that draws `ThemeScope`, and `src/App.tsx` routes it in the accessibility test build only, so the production build holds no `ThemeScope`, no live or heritage stylesheet and no font file of either look, before the step and after it; the chunk is checked in `dist-a11y-on` and `dist-a11y-off` (see the step's Build).
- **The tests that move (step 30).** Found: `test/unit/theme-scope.test.tsx` and `test/unit/theme-scope-edges.test.tsx` draw heritage scopes and mocked the live file only, so a heritage scope would now import the real `heritage.css`, which the unit runner, processing no stylesheet, gives as an empty module; both now mock and count it beside the live file. `test/unit/theme-scope-wiring.test.ts` held the live file as the only one `ThemeScope` imports; it now holds both, each lazily, and its dash and tool name check covers `heritage.css`.
- **index.css (step 30).** Confirmed: `test/unit/tokens.test.ts` pins the `@import` lines of `src/index.css` and scans every file under `src`, `heritage.css` included, comments included, for the names of monospace faces, so `heritage.css` names none and `src/index.css` is not changed.
- **The date line (step 30).** Found: "step 30: heritage fonts; " in front of step 29's entry would have moved step 18 to character 417, past the 400 characters the older record tests read, so step 27's entry is shortened to "step 27: chess data and tables" and step 18 now ends at character 398. `test/unit/redesign-status-step24.test.ts` allowed only steps 25 to 29 in front of its own entry; its pattern now lets a step in the 30s come first, as those of steps 25 to 29 already did.
- **The planned line's place (step 30).** Found: the planned line "Small caps (step 30)" is under "Planned for later steps" in the Deviations, beside the planned lines of steps 29 and 31, not in the Decisions; it keeps its words there, with Done in step 30 added.
- **The production stylesheet before the step (step 30).** Found: at `abe7d07` the production stylesheet is 127,940 bytes, 44 more than step 29's 127,896, because the commit between the two steps wrote a selector into `test/a11y/baseline.ts` that the stylesheet build reads as a class with a variant and adds a rule for. The step's own files add none.
- **The baseline (step 30).** Confirmed: `test/a11y/baseline.ts` reads `/`, `/tournaments`, `/membership` and `/tournaments/fx-t-done` on `a11y-off`, none of which draws `ThemeScope` or imports anything the step adds, and `src/index.css` gains no import, so no face changes there; the comparison matched in every run, and `test/a11y/baseline.json` keeps its hash (`ce04ac06f0b3906739738413ef7aa3a21b0e8b0b9e3fe1c09dbca15f9f4dcbef`). `baseline.ts` and `baseline.json` are not changed.
- **Lint, build and the browser runs (step 30).** Measured: lint 13 errors and 13 warnings before and after; the build sizes and the browser-test builds' files are under the step's Build; the browser runs under Checks.
- **Counts (step 30).** The raw colour allowlist stays at 35 entries and 49 uses, `test/a11y/known-violations.json` keeps its 18 entries, the fixture page's 13 `[data-status]` elements and five Buttons are as they were, and the step adds no browser test. The unit count at `abe7d07` is 4000 in 146 files, one more than step 29 recorded: the commit between the steps gave one `it.each` in `test/unit/e2e-preview-guard.test.ts` a sixth case.
- **The retry after a failed load (step 30).** Found: `ThemeScope` forgets an import that fails, so the next scope of that look calls `import()` again, and step 22's Fonts line says such an import "is retried by the next one". The call is made, but in a built site the file is not fetched again: Vite's preload helper (`preload-helper-*.js` in the browser-test builds) records a stylesheet's address before it adds the `<link>`, never forgets it, and leaves a `<link>` that failed in the page, so a second `import()` of the stylesheet resolves at once with no request, and the file stays missing until the page is reloaded: a heritage page then keeps the base type for the rest of the visit, the accent class's Instrument Serif included, since the rule that switches it off is in the file. Driven in jsdom, the helper the `a11y-on` build emits rejected the first request with "Unable to preload CSS for" the heritage stylesheet's address and resolved the second with no new `<link>`, one before and one after, while a stylesheet it had not tried still got one; `test/unit/heritage-edges.test.ts` holds that against the helper of the browser-test build it makes. The unit tests that fail an import mock the file, so they show the call only, and their names now say `import()`; the comment beside the call in `ThemeScope.tsx` and the step's ThemeScope bullet say the file stays missing. The live file has the same gap. Step 22's Fonts line is left as it was written; step 31's loader handles a failed load itself (see Follow-ups, "The heritage stylesheet before the first paint (step 30)").

### Follow-ups

- **AC9 live check.** After the first push, K opens the branch deploy in the Pages dashboard and confirms its D1 binding shows `lca-db-preview`. Record the result here.
- **First preview migration.** K runs `npm run db:migrate:preview` once before reviewing the first preview; on the new, empty database it applies every migration from `0001`.
- **Preview secrets.** Before the first push, K checks the Preview environment's secrets in the Pages dashboard and removes any live Stripe or Resend key left from before this slice, then sets Stripe test keys before anyone pays on a preview.
- **Club logos.** Previews write to the production `lca-club-logos` bucket. A separate preview bucket would need creating and binding under `[env.preview]`.
- **Logins.** Previews use the production Supabase project, so a sign-in on a preview is a real account.
- **One preview database for every branch.** A migration applied from one branch is there for all of them; only the redesign branch migrates it today.
- **Section move loses the early, member and late adjustments (step 10, from review).** When an entry changes section, its pending payment is set to the new section's regular fee, as before. A player who entered Open at $25 with a $5 early discount (payment $20) and is moved to Reserve at $15 is charged $15, not $10. Kept as it was because acceptance criterion 4 asks for identical charges. Later, work out the change as `priceEntry` for the new section minus `priceEntry` for the old one, using the tiers that applied when the entry was made.
- **Bye rounds are not checked as whole numbers on a single entry (step 10, from review).** `registrations.ts` accepts `byeRounds: [1.5]`, and the waitlist branch saves bye rounds before the bye limits are checked; `batch.ts` does check whole numbers. Make `byeRoundsSchema` `z.array(z.number().int())` in `domain/contracts/registration.ts` and move the bye checks above the capacity check.
- **A section's own early price needs one rule (step 10, from review, for WS08).** `priceEntry` applies a section's `feeEarly` only while `early_deadline` is set and not passed; `tierFees` in `domain/events/sections.ts` reports it with no deadline check. No row has `fee_early` until WS08; decide the rule then and apply it in both. A `feeEarly` above the base would also give a positive line labelled "Early entry discount".
- **Two more "USCF ID" messages (step 10).** Walk-ins done in step 11: both refusals in `walk-ins.ts` say "US Chess ID". `functions/api/uscf/lookup.ts` ("A valid 8-digit USCF ID is required") still says USCF, and `src/pages/TournamentManagePage.tsx` shows a bare ISO date in a tooltip.
- **Ids that can collide (step 10).** Registration ids are `reg-<tournament>-<Date.now() base36>`, so two different players entering in the same millisecond would collide on the primary key. This predates step 10 and is untested.
- **Walk-ins and the waitlist (step 11).** Done in step 11: neither reads the JSON, a walk-in sets its `section_id`, and the waitlist prices with `priceEntry` over the row. `entryPrice` stays an adapter over the JSON for the tournament page and the family entry panel until step 13. `functions/api/admin/tournaments/[id]/manage.ts` still reads the JSON for the manage page's section list (out of this step's scope); the writer and the triggers keep it in step with the live rows.
- **Walk-in rating accepts fractions (step 11, from review).** `walkInRequestSchema` takes any number for `uscfRating`, but `walkInResponseSchema` needs `rating_at_entry` to be a whole number, so a rating of 1500.5 is saved and echoed back in a body that breaks its own contract. The old handler accepted it too. Add `.int()` with the message "Enter the rating as a whole number, or leave it blank." and one 400 case in `walkins-waitlist.test.ts`, and record it as a deviation.
- **Brief text.** Update brief 0.1 rule 8 and the 0.2 line "K applies remote migrations" in the next brief revision.
- **Member discount query.** K runs the production query under Decisions before checkpoint B merges; record the result here. Not run yet (step 15 lists it with the two other things the merge waits for).
- **Production before the merge (step 15). Done.** The member-discount query, the step 8 queries and the legacy-shape queries were run on production on Monday, October 12, 2026; results under Checkpoint B. 0052 and 0053 are applied; `migrate-db.yml` applies 0054 on the merge.
- **Enforcement at checkout (step 15).** WS06's household checkout calls `needsMembershipAtCheckout`, adds the right membership in the same payment, and only then may `membershipRequirementSwitch` be turned on. WS08 replaces `FORCE_LCA_MEMBERSHIP` with the admin-only site setting `membership_required_for_all`, and builds the full setup toggle.
- **New refusals of typed input (checkpoint B review).** A negative section fee or a section name over 80 characters, typed in the wizard or the setup page, is now refused, and the director sees only the generic "Some fields are missing or not valid." These are not on the decided list. Later, show which field is wrong.
- **Pre-submit expiry line (checkpoint B review).** The event page's line before entering still shows the US Chess expiry as "2026-09-01", while the warning after entering shows "Tue, Sep 1, 2026". Show the weekday form in both.
- **Leftover USCF wording (checkpoint B review).** `TournamentDetailPage.tsx` still says "This is a USCF-rated tournament. Add your USCF ID..." in the Register click message and "USCF ID" in the confirmation dialog, and `FamilyRegistrationPanel.tsx` says "needs a USCF ID". Change them to "US Chess" with matching test text.
- **Production reads before the merge (checkpoint B review).** The legacy-shape queries, the member-discount query and the step 8 queries are listed under Checkpoint B, Before the merge. Not run yet.
- **Stale detach race (step 15, from review). Closed in step 15.** When an admin took the club off an event, the write that forced `requires_lca_membership` to 1 depended on the value read at the start of the request, so a rep's concurrent switch-off could have left an event with no club and the column at 0. `admin/tournaments/[id].ts` now writes 1 whenever the event has no club after the save, whatever the earlier read said.
- **Concurrent edits lose fields (step 15, from testing; predates the step).** `PATCH /api/admin/tournaments/[id]` rewrites most columns from the row it read, so two saves at the same moment can lose the other's change to the name, the club and similar fields while both answer 200. Only `rounds` and `requires_lca_membership` are written when sent. Consider writing only the changed columns, or comparing `updated_at`.
- **Migration header (step 15, from review).** The generated header of `0054_requires_lca_membership.sql` still says to edit the schema and generate again rather than edit the file; the paragraph below it explains the hand-written update. Reword the first sentence when the file is next touched.
- **Clearinghouse dates.** `src/lib/clearinghouse.ts` keeps its own `formatDate` ("Oct 24, 2026", no weekday), used only by `src/pages/HomePage.tsx` for partner events. It should move to `formatDate` from `@domain/format` (with the year option) when the homepage is rebuilt behind `newHome` in WS03, and then be removed. Step 29: the file is `src/areas/events/clearinghouse.ts` since step 17, and the home page imports it at line 10.
- **Reading stored timestamps (step 3).** Any later step that shows a `datetime('now')` column (`created_at`, `updated_at`, `sent_at`, `recorded_at` and the like) with `formatDate` or `formatTime` must pass `{ stored: 'utc' }`; the default reads wall-clock text and would show those times 5 to 6 hours early. Reviewers of those steps check for it.
- **One time-zone definition (step 3).** Done in step 4: `domain/format/centralTime.ts` takes `LCA_TIME_ZONE` from `CENTRAL_TIME_ZONE` in `date.ts`. `src/pages/AnnualMeetingPage.tsx` still writes `'America/Chicago'` in its own date formatter; it should use `formatDate` and `formatTime` when that page moves to the heritage look.
- **Minor names on the entrants list (step 4).** `publicName` is ready but unused. WS06 calls it from the public event endpoint once guardian links and the under-18 mark on an entry exist, with `'entrants'` for the pre-event list and `'results'` everywhere else.
- **Name suffixes (step 4, from review).** `publicName` skips Jr., Sr., II, III and IV but not a trailing "V" or "3rd", so "Leo Robichaux V" shows as "Leo V." on the entrants list. The result is shorter, not a leak. Add `v`, `2nd` and `3rd` to the suffix list with a test when `publicName` is wired in WS06.
- **Zero increment or delay (step 3).** `formatTimeControl("G/90+0")` reads "... + 0 sec per move". Minor; drop the clause when the value is 0 when a page first shows time controls.
- **Dynamic imports (step 3).** ESLint's `no-restricted-imports` does not see `import()` or `require()` inside `domain/`; `test/unit/domain-boundaries.test.ts` scans for both.
- **Event-mode phases in WS02 (step 5).** The endpoint computes `lastRoundResultsComplete` (a non-null result in every section's last scheduled round) and passes it with `firstRoundTimesFromSchedule(round_schedule, date, end_date)`. It builds the hide key with `phaseKey(id, phase, round)`, passing the first round of the next playing day for `week` and `dayBefore` and the first round of that day for `checkin` and `eventDay`, so a strip hidden before day 1 comes back before round 2. Its `pollSeconds` rule (20 in `checkin`, `eventDay` and the round phases, 300 otherwise), the sooner-first sort and their tests are WS02's. When WS02 or WS07 changes the inputs, only `domain/events/eventMode.ts` and its test change. `eventPhase` can join the one-definition list in `test/unit/domain-shims.test.ts` once WS02 calls it, so a second copy fails the suite.
- **jsdom 30.** Moving to jsdom 30 needs Node 22.22.2 or later in development and in CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` asks for `'22'`). Not needed for WS01.
- **drizzle-kit and added foreign keys (step 6).** Until drizzle-kit writes `ON DELETE` for a column added with `ALTER TABLE`, add such columns with `npm run db:generate -- <name> --custom` and write the clause by hand. When drizzle-kit is upgraded, run `npx drizzle-kit generate` with no schema change first; it must still report no changes.
- **Development audit findings (step 6).** drizzle-kit brings older esbuild copies (0.18.20 through `@esbuild-kit`, and 0.25.12), which `npm audit` reports as moderate (the esbuild development server advisory). They run only on a developer's machine and are not in the built site or the functions.
- **`db:generate` arguments (step 6, from review).** Every argument except `--custom` is taken as the migration name, so `--name add_notes` gives `0052_name_add_notes.sql` and `--help` is read as a name. Reject other `--` arguments and print the usage line.
- **Bracketed identifiers (step 6, from testing).** `splitSql` does not treat `[name]` as quoted, so a `;` inside one would split. No migration uses them and drizzle-kit never writes them; the old splitter had the same gap.
- **The import check (step 6).** Done in step 9: the check in `test/unit/schema-drift.test.ts` that no runtime code imports `functions/db` is removed, now that the admin tournament endpoints use `getDb`.
- **Production and the migrations (step 6).** The schema was introspected from the migrations, not from the production database. Anything ever changed in production by hand, outside `migrations/`, is not in it.
- **Endpoints that do not answer with JSON (step 7).** `GET clubs/[id]/logo`, `GET officer-photos/[key]` and `GET post-images/[id]` serve images, `GET admin/members/export` serves CSV text, and `POST stripe/webhook` takes Stripe's event body. They are on the pending list, marked. When each is next changed, decide whether it gets a contract for its error answers only or moves to the excluded list with that reason.
- **Page route tests (step 7).** No test runs `functions/tournaments/[id].ts`, `functions/clubs/[id].ts` or `functions/news/[slug].ts`. A test that checks the link-preview tags for a visible event, a hidden draft and an unknown id belongs with the next change to `functions/utils/linkPreview.ts`.
- **Contracts as endpoints change (step 7).** Each step that changes an endpoint adds its contract to `domain/contracts`, a contract test in `test/integration/contracts/` that uses `contracts['<route>'].<METHOD>`, moves the handler to `parseBody` where it reads a body, takes the line out of `test/unit/contracts-pending.ts` and lowers `PENDING_COUNT`. Request schemas are written from the bodies `src/lib/api.ts` sends.
- **CORS methods (step 7).** `functions/utils/response.ts` allows `GET, POST, PATCH, OPTIONS`, while three handlers take PUT and eighteen take DELETE. Same-origin calls are unaffected; worth correcting when the file is next changed for another reason.
- **Review nits (step 7).** (1) The `expectContract` comment says a loose object is caught for extra fields; it is not, only `z.object` is, so response schemas must be `z.object` or `z.strictObject`. (2) `contract-coverage.test.ts` treats a bare `onRequest` as a catch-all handler; a `_middleware.ts` under `functions/api` would need skipping first. (3) `parseBody` fields use zod's default wording (for example "Invalid input: expected string, received undefined"); request schemas should carry their own plain messages, or `parseBody` should map issue codes, before any form shows them next to inputs.
- **Production queries for step 8.** K runs the read-only queries under step 8 before checkpoint A merges and the four after it, and again before checkpoint B merges; record the answers here.
- **Renames from the setup form (step 13).** Until the wizard and the manage page send each section's id back, a rename there is a removal plus an addition and is refused while the section has entries (decision 7). Done in step 13: the pages keep and send each loaded section's id, and `sections-rename-ui-path.test.ts` shows a rename in that body keeping its entries. Neither screen has a rename box yet (see Deviations, step 13).
- **Games by name.** Games still name their section; the writer moves them on a rename from a live row. A `section_id` on `tournament_games` (WS07) would let the rename skip that step.
- **Report settings past 8000 characters.** A rename that would push `report_settings` past the 8000 characters the PATCH keeps leaves the old key in place rather than cutting the JSON. Nothing that long exists today.
- **Two edits at once.** The writer plans from the rows it reads, then writes in one batch that opens with guards. If another save (or old code writing the JSON) changed the rows in between, or a section being removed gained an entry or a game, the guard or the live-name index fails the batch as a whole and the handler answers 409 with a plain message; the director reloads and saves again. The guard does not cover a change to `report_settings` alone between the read and the batch; the PATCH writes `report_settings` as it was sent or as it read it, as it did before this step.
- **Guard depends on D1's SQLite (step 9, from review).** The stale-plan guard relies on `group_concat` following the order of its ordered subquery, and `isSectionsConflict` recognises the guard by the text `bad JSON path:` (SQLite 3.45 and later wording). Both fail safe, nothing is written, but on a SQLite build that differs every section save would answer 409 (order) or 500 (error text) until the code changes. D1's version could not be read (`sqlite_version()` is blocked). Re-check when D1's engine changes; matching the older "JSON path error" wording is optional.
- **Site code may import `functions/db` (step 9, from review).** Removing the import check from `schema-drift.test.ts` also dropped it for `src/`. A narrowed check that walks `src/` only and expects no import of `functions/db` or `drizzle` should be restored, so the site bundle stays free of Drizzle. `domain/` is covered by `domain-boundaries.test.ts`.
- **Bare-name section pinned to the event fee (step 12, from review).** A section with no fee of its own is answered with `entryFee` set to the event fee; the manage page echoes it, so a later section save stores that fee as the section's own and the section stops following the event's `entry_fee`. Nothing edits the event entry fee after creation today, so no price is wrong yet. Smallest fix in `buildSaveSections`: when the input carries `fees.regular` (an echoed answer), the row's `fee_regular` is null and `entryFee === fees.regular`, keep `fee_regular` null. Step 13 left it (the writer was outside that step's files, and the pages' behaviour is unchanged); owner WS08, before directors can change the event fee.
- **Public responses expose personal and internal fields (step 12, from review; predates the step).** `GET /api/clubs/[id]` returns every officer's email address, and `GET /api/tournaments` returns `keep_apart` (member ids), `created_by` and `report_settings` for every visible event through `t.*`. The new strict contracts now treat these as promised shape. Trim them from the responses and their contracts, and add a role-safety contract case, in a separate task.
- **Admin PATCH heals the mirror from itself (step 12, from testing).** The admin PATCH writes `round_schedule` on every call, which fires the 0053 update trigger and re-reads the JSON mirror into the section rows. A mirror behind the table is therefore overwritten on the next admin PATCH; this matters only if something writes the rows without the mirror. Resolved when the triggers go with the JSON columns. Changed in step 14: an edit that sends neither sections nor round times no longer writes a JSON column, so it no longer fires the trigger or heals the mirror; the sections and schedule saves each write the mirror last.
- **Audit argument match (step 12, from testing).** The reader audit stops its argument match at the first `)`, so `parseJsonArray(await load(id).then((r) => r.sections))` is not flagged. Judged contrived; tighten if a reader like it appears.
- **Manage-page readers (step 11 follow-up).** Done in step 12: `manage.ts` reads the table.
- **Client readers (step 13).** Done in step 13: the pages read the contract-derived section type, and `entryPrice` and `sectionBaseFee` are gone.
- **Schedules in WS06 and WS08 (step 14).** WS06 adds the schedule picker and sets `registrations.schedule_id` (today the trigger sets the primary). WS08's setup builds the second-schedule screen and must send labels when it switches the primary. The default labels read awkwardly in messages ("the “Main schedule” schedule"); drop the trailing word when the label already ends in "schedule" when WS08 shows them to directors.
- **Retiring the JSON columns.** When `tournaments.sections` and `round_schedule` are dropped, the four 0053 triggers go in the same migration.
- **`tournament_games.section_id` (WS07).** Games keep their section names; the archived rows the backfill makes for game names are there for that link.
- **Member discount line (step 15).** Done in step 15: `priceEntry` no longer reads `member_discount`. The column stays; drop it, `memberDiscount` in the edit request and `member_discount` in the contract row in a later migration once production has run without it.
- **`entryPrice` on the pages (step 13).** The tournament page and the family entry panel still price from the legacy JSON through `entryPrice`. Done in step 13: both price with `priceShownSection`, which passes a section's own early or late price on to `priceEntry`.
- **An own early price of $0 under a larger discount (step 13).** An answer's `fees.early` of $0 is either the section's own price or a worked-out one clamped at $0 (the early discount larger than the fee), and `priceShownSection` takes it as worked out. The page then differs from checkout only when the late fee is also due (the early and late windows overlap). The 33 opaque ones sit with a selected navy fill (`border-lca-navy bg-brand-navy text-white`) and should follow it (`border-brand-navy`); the hover and tint borders (`/10` to `/50`) sit on light grounds and should become `border-ink` at the same opacity; the impersonation banner's Exit border sits on gold and stays navy. The same tint of the ink would lighten the ground instead, but Tailwind gives a token class with an opacity modifier only its token at full strength as the fallback for browsers that cannot mix colours (Safari 15.4 to 16.1, Chrome 109), which with `newLook` off would turn each tint into a solid navy box behind navy text. Step 24 moves the tints and the tinted navy borders to the ink together, with a fallback of their own: an `@supports not (color: color-mix(in lab, red, red))` block that gives each tint in use the sRGB tint its brand class falls back to, or tint tokens whose plain `:root` value is that tint. Done in step 24: the 17 tints are `bg-ink` at their opacity, and the `@supports` block gives each the tint `bg-brand-navy` fell back to; the hero photo's veil, the navy buttons' hover and the Exit button's hover on gold stay navy.

The next run passed. A retry on that test, or a database folder per server, would end it. Step 26: two of its four full runs failed `baseline.ts` the same way, on `/tournaments/fx-t-done`, whose data request got 500 while a server logged `SQLITE_BUSY`; the page drew its error message, so the comparison reported its Button changed and its table header and figures missing, though no colour had changed. In the step's runs each lock came within seconds of the `a11y-off` project's start, when its server first reads the database. The run after passed, and so did the step's last run, after the fixes review asked for, with one more lock logged at the same moment. Since a failed baseline reads as a changed colour, a database folder per server (a copy of `.wrangler/a11y` for each, made after the seed) is worth making before the next step adds browser tests. Step 27: while its spec was written, one run of it failed two tests the same way, the pages drawing their error after a `SQLITE_BUSY`; the spec now loads a page that drew its error once more, and of the step's two full runs the first logged no lock and the second one, while other specs ran, with no test failing. The whole run again, the seventh, logged none and passed. Of the step's own specs, `brand.spec.ts` reads `/about`, which asks the server for no data of its own, and the fixture page, each once what it reads is drawn, and `brand-edges.spec.ts` reads only the header and the footer of the twelve pages it opens, which a page's failed data request does not change. Step 29: of its full runs, the first failed `baseline.ts` on `/tournaments/fx-t-done` after a server logged `SQLITE_BUSY` twice, the page drawing its empty state (the plain Button, and no table header or figures), and the second on `/tournaments`, whose Button was missing after one `SQLITE_BUSY`; in both `/` matched, no colour or font had changed and `test/a11y/baseline.json` was not rewritten. The third, once the step's records were written, failed `baseline.ts` on `/tournaments/fx-t-done` the same way after one `SQLITE_BUSY`; run on its own after a fresh database, `baseline.ts` matched. The two full runs after those passed: in the first a server logged one `SQLITE_BUSY` while the baseline comparison ran, which matched, and the second logged none. Done before step 30: `test/a11y/global-setup.ts` applies the migrations and the seed to a folder for each server, `.wrangler/a11y/on` for `a11y-on` (port 8801) and `.wrangler/a11y/off` for `a11y-off` (port 8802), and `playwright.config.ts` serves each build on its own, so the two `wrangler pages dev` processes never open the same SQLite file; the seed, the migrations and the fake secrets are as they were. `npm run perf:home` builds and serves `.wrangler/a11y/on` only, as its header says. - **Quoted strings in comment prose (step 23).** The script searches a comment only for its quoted strings (commented-out code), but it takes whatever lies between two matching quote marks on one line as one. So in a comment line with two apostrophes, such as "We don't want text-lca-navy here, it's dark mode's job.", the class between them is rewritten, which the script's header says never happens. This tree has no such line: the seven comment rewrites are commented-out JSX in `NewsPage.tsx` and `AnnualMeetingPage.tsx`. Step 24 runs the same script with a second map, so before that run the quote match should open and close a string only where no letter or digit stands on the outer side of the quote mark, with a test in `test/unit/semantic-colours-edges.test.ts` that puts a watched class between "don't" and "it's" in a comment and expects no rewrite. Done in step 24, with that test.

- **K's lab run on the preview (step 29).** AC7's second half: K runs Lighthouse's mobile check on `/` of a deployed preview, where Cloudflare serves the site with its own compression and caching rather than `wrangler pages dev` on one computer, and writes its score, its runs and its LCP element in the AC7 row beside the local ones (82, 78 and 79, median 79, and 78, 81 and 71, median 78). A branch preview serves the site with `newLook` off: `src/lib/features.ts` reads `VITE_FLAGS` only in a build made with `--mode a11y`, and a preview is built without it, so its `/` is today's five-slide slideshow, 2,171,020 bytes of photos drawn as backgrounds, with one of them as its LCP element. K's run of the still hero waits for a preview built with `newLook` on, or for the switch being turned on; a run before then is the figure from before the step, worth writing down as that, and not a check of this step. `npm run perf:home` measures only the local build. The local runs measured the Facebook band in its error state, since the test build has no page token (see "What the lab check found beyond the hero (step 29)"); a run on the preview measures the band as visitors see it only where the preview holds the Facebook page token and page id, or a feed cache an earlier answer filled, so write down which the band showed: its posts, or "Couldn't load posts right now."
- **What the lab check found beyond the hero (step 29).** For WS03, which rebuilds the home page. In every run of `npm run perf:home` the LCP element was the hero's picture, fetched at high priority, and Lighthouse found these beyond it. The picture is not discoverable in the first HTML ("Request is discoverable in initial document: no"): the page draws itself from its script, so it asks for the picture only once the main script has run (509,976 bytes in the test build Lighthouse measured last, about 144,773 transferred, and 509,728 in the site's own build), of which Lighthouse counts about 84 KiB unused at the first paint. The stylesheet (about 22 KB transferred) blocks the first paint. The layout shifts by 0 to 0.36, against the brief's limit of 0.1, as the tournaments and clubs columns grow when `/api/clearinghouse?upcoming=true` and `/api/clubs` answer and the featured line appears above them, and as the web font swaps under the hero's heading; Lighthouse names the footer's logo as an unsized image beside the shifts, though it carries a width and a height and Lighthouse's own check of unsized images passes. The logo file, 400 pixels wide, is drawn at 56, about 15 KB it could save. A hero the first HTML names (or a preload for its picture), a fixed space for the lists and the featured line, and the lists' data sent with the page would answer most of it. The lab build measures the Facebook band in its error state, because the test build has no page token: its servers hold `FACEBOOK_PAGE_TOKEN` and `FACEBOOK_PAGE_ID` empty, and migration 0023 seeds the feed cache with no posts and no time, so `/api/facebook-posts?limit=5` answers 502 at once (in 13 to 52 ms in each of the six runs whose full report was kept before the img's fallback changed, and in 25 to 46 ms in each of the three kept since) and the band, between the tournaments and the clubs, draws one line, "Couldn't load posts right now.", with its link to the page. On the site the band's request goes through the function to Facebook (which marks its answer to be kept for 30 minutes) and the band grows from "Loading…" to as many as five posts, so neither that server call nor that growth is in the scores or the layout shift above. That check is WS03's, and K's on the preview (see "K's lab run on the preview (step 29)").
- **WS03 deletes HeroSlideshow (step 29).** When WS03 replaces the hero it deletes `HeroSlideshow` and the slideshow's half of `HomeHero` (the track of slides and the controls), and with them: the `HomePage.tsx` entry of the allowlist in `test/unit/no-rotation.test.ts` (its two calls); the slide controls' four entries in `test/unit/fixtures/raw-colours-allowlist.json` (`bg-white/20`, `bg-white/40`, `hover:bg-white/30` and `hover:bg-white/60`, six uses), and the hero's other two, the Join LCA button's `bg-white/10` and `hover:bg-white/20`, if the new hero drops them; the dots' six `target-size` entries a build in `test/a11y/known-violations.json`; the flag-off half of `test/unit/hero-no-autoplay.test.tsx` and `test/unit/fixtures/home-hero-today.ts`; the two flag-off timer tests in `test/unit/hero-images-edges.test.tsx` (the slideshow's one timer going with the page once its controls are used, and one timer under strict mode); the `a11y-off` tests of today's slideshow in `test/a11y/home.spec.ts`, and the `a11y-off` run of the failing-lists test in `test/a11y/home-edges.spec.ts`, which holds the slideshow on the page clock and reads its hero; the pins in `test/unit/hero-images-edges.test.tsx` of `test/a11y/baseline.json`'s sha256 and of its sample of `/` (the hero's "Find a tournament"), which move with the baseline when WS03 records it again on purpose; and the imports of the photos of slides 2 to 5, which take 1,601,855 bytes out of every build; slide 1's JPEG (569,165 bytes) stays as long as a picture of slide 1 needs the fallback `ResponsiveImage` asks for, which a browser that shows AVIF or WebP never fetches.
- **The Facebook band's image with no width or height (step 29; older than the step).** `src/components/FacebookFeed.tsx` line 212 draws a post's picture in its full variant, on `/news`, lazy and with no width or height, so the page can move when it arrives; `/` draws the compact variant, which has no picture. Left as it is: WS10, which rebuilds the news page, or step 32's sweep gives it its size.
- **The header's and the footer's images with newLook off (step 29; older than the step).** With `newLook` off the header's logo (`src/components/layout/Navbar.tsx` line 240) and the footer's (`src/components/layout/Footer.tsx` line 62) are sized by their classes and carry no width or height attributes, today's markup, which this step leaves as it is (`test/a11y/home.spec.ts` measures it on `a11y-off`); under `newLook` the header draws the rook mark and the footer `LcaLogo`, with both. They go when `newLook` is turned on.
- **Making the hero pictures again (step 29).** After a slide photo changes, `npm run images` (`node scripts/images.mjs`) writes the 30 files again and stops when one is over 200,000 bytes; lower that file's quality in the script's table, run it again, and commit what it writes. It was written with sharp 0.34.5, the version `package.json` pins and wrangler's local runtime uses; 0.35.5 exists (published Sun, Sep 27, 2026) and may write files of other sizes, so after an upgrade run it, run `test/unit/image-budget.test.ts` and commit what changes.
- **K reviews the step 30 judgement calls (step 30).** As with steps 23 to 29: before `newLook` and `themeHeritage` are turned on, K reads the judgement calls under step 30 (the budget counted over the roman latin files, with the italic apart and 126,576 bytes on a page with italic text, and whether to keep the italic; the latin files only; Caslon only through the display hook, which carries its own size, 36 pixels unless a page asks for more; the display size registered with `@property` outside the guard; weight 400 on display headings; the 37em measure; Capsize's widths for the fallback faces; the accent class in the scope; rules in no layer; the `data-heritage` hooks; Source Serif 5.3.0) and looks at a heritage page once step 31 draws one, on a phone and on a wide screen, in the light look and in dark mode.
- **The fallback faces measured (step 30).** The fallback faces' numbers are worked out from Georgia's published metrics and the face files, not measured. Once `HeritageLayout` draws `/about` and `/governance/bylaws` in the look, step 31 measures the layout shift across the swap there (AC11, under 0.1), from the start of the navigation with the stylesheet's own arrival (see "The heritage stylesheet before the first paint (step 30)"), and tunes the numbers if it must. Bold and semibold text in the fallback take the regular face's numbers, though Georgia Bold is wider (an average width of 1062 against 913). Where Georgia is not installed, as on Android phones, ChromeOS and most Linux systems, both fallback faces are skipped and the swap runs from the device's own serif, at its own size and metrics, so step 31 measures AC11 twice, once with a face that has Georgia's metrics and once with no Georgia, as an Android phone draws it (see "Georgia on the test computers (step 30)").
- **Hooks for the heritage pages (step 30).** The heritage type reaches only elements that carry its hooks. Step 31's `HeritageLayout` and the pages it wraps put `data-heritage="display"` on their h1 and h2 (through `PageHero` or the pages), `figure` on display figures such as "1915" and "300+", and `card`, `table` and `mat` on the boxes that take the 2px radius; WS14's bylaws and minutes reader puts `reader` on its text column; WS10's honour board heads take `.small-caps`. Body text outside a reader keeps the site's face until a step gives it a hook of its own. A page that sets `--display-size` sets it in rem or px or as a percentage, never as a bare number or in a unit of the heading's own font (the stylesheet's header says why, and `test/unit/heritage-css.test.ts` checks every value set under `src`).
- **Georgia on the test computers (step 30).** Georgia is not installed on the computer that built this step, so a browser test there draws the device's serif while a heritage face loads, never the fallback faces. Step 31's layout shift check runs where Georgia is installed, or installs a face with Georgia's metrics for the test (Gelasio, which its designer made metrically compatible with Georgia, is one), and says which. Georgia is missing on many visitors' devices as well: Android phones, ChromeOS and most Linux systems have none, so there `local('Georgia')` finds nothing, both fallback faces are skipped, and the swap runs from the device's own serif (Noto Serif on Android), at its own size and metrics, to Caslon and Source Serif. The spec's budget is for a mid-tier phone (section 2.4, CLS under 0.1), so step 31 measures AC11 twice, once with a face that has Georgia's metrics and once with no Georgia, as an Android phone draws it. If the swap without Georgia is over 0.1, step 31 adds a second fallback face for each real face on Android's serif (`local('Noto Serif')`), its numbers worked out the same way, after the Georgia one in each stack.
- **Source Serif 4 5.3.1 (step 30).** `@fontsource-variable/source-serif-4` 5.3.1 was published Sunday, October 11, 2026, and is two weeks old on Sunday, October 25, 2026. A later step may move to it then, with `npm install --save-exact`; `test/unit/heritage-budget.test.ts` and `test/unit/heritage-glyphs.test.ts` check its latin files' sizes and glyphs.
- **Letters outside the latin files (step 30).** Inside the heritage type a letter outside the latin files, in a Vietnamese or a Polish name for one, is drawn from Georgia or the device's serif, since only the latin files are named. Both packages ship a latin-ext file and Source Serif 4 a vietnamese one, which a browser would fetch only for such letters; step 31, WS10 (Champions) or WS14 (Board, Minutes) adds them if a name needs them.
- **The heritage stylesheet before the first paint (step 30).** `ThemeScope` asks for `src/styles/themes/heritage.css` in an effect, after the scope's first paint, and the file holds the font tokens, the type rules and the fallback faces as well as the faces (see Deviations, Step 30), so an element with a hook is drawn first in the base type and moves when the file arrives. A check in Chromium on the `a11y-on` build, served as files at 390 by 844 pixels with the stylesheet held back one second and the font files refused, so that no face could swap, and the hooks put on the fixture page's elements before its first paint: the h1 with the display hook painted at 30 pixels in Geist at 700 and became the Caslon stack at 36 pixels and 400 when the file arrived, a layout shift of 0.0145, and with a reading column of six paragraphs added the shift was 0.0964, nearly all of AC11's 0.1, before any face swapped. Step 31 loads the file before heritage content paints, and measures AC11 from the start of the navigation with the stylesheet held back as well as the faces, not only across the font swap. One way: `ThemeScope` keeps one promise of the import, which Vite's loader resolves once the stylesheet has loaded, and `HeritageLayout`'s lazy route waits for it. It must be the one promise, since Vite's loader answers a second request for a stylesheet it has already added at once, before the stylesheet has loaded, and a function exported beside the component draws the `react-refresh/only-export-components` lint warning, so the loader may need a module of its own, with the single-importer pins of `test/unit/theme-scope-wiring.test.ts` moved to it. That loader must also handle a failed load itself rather than call `import()` again, since Vite's loader answers a second request for a stylesheet that failed at once and fetches nothing, and leaves the failed `<link>` in the page (see Verify items, "The retry after a failed load (step 30)"). Two ways: take the failed `<link>` out and add a fresh one, its address from the file's `?url` import, or add one whose address carries a query that sets it apart from the one that failed. The live file has the same gap, which WS07 meets when it binds the live fonts; step 22's Fonts line, which says a failed import is retried by the next scope, holds for `ThemeScope`'s call and not for the file. The check in Chromium also found a shift that is not the look's: on a page the site loads on demand, the footer moves when the page takes the place of the loading line, a layout shift of 0.67 to 0.69 in all on `/about` and `/governance/bylaws` at 390 by 844 pixels and 0.22 to 0.35 at 1280 by 800, on both browser-test builds, so with `newLook` off as well, against 0.0057 to 0.0066 on `/`, which loads with the site (each `/api/` request answered 404). Step 31 keeps that shift apart from the look's, or holds the page's height while it loads, and says which.

## WS02: Information architecture and navigation

**Status:** not started. **Branch:** none. **PR:** none.

- Read the brief with this path: the event-mode phase machine is `domain/events/eventMode.ts` (built in WS01, step 5), not `functions/utils/eventMode.ts`. `functions/api/me/event-mode.ts` imports `eventPhase`, `phaseKey` and `firstRoundTimesFromSchedule(schedule, date, endDate)` from it (`../../../domain/events/eventMode` from the server, `@domain/events/eventMode` in the site). The endpoint passes a round to `phaseKey` for `week` and `dayBefore` (the first round of the next playing day, 1 before day 1) and for `checkin` and `eventDay` (the first round of that day), because those phases come back on later days of a multi-day event and a key ending in `:0` would keep a hidden strip hidden. The endpoint, `src/lib/eventModeCopy.ts`, the strip, `pollSeconds` and the sooner-first sort are still WS02's. See the WS01 deviations "Event-mode phases (step 5)" and "Event-mode phase times (step 5)" (`eventDay` runs until 6:00 AM, not 11:59 PM) and the decision "Event-mode phase gaps (step 5)" (including days with no round).
